.

B.5
Predicate Folding and Unfolding

The premise
p(\vec{x}_{\text{in}};\vec{x}_{\text{out}})~\{\bigvee_{i\in I}(\exists\vec{x}_{i}.~A_{i})\}\in\mathsf{Preds}
is required for the rules below.

\frac{\llbracket\vec{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\pi})\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{\theta}\triangleq[\vec{x}_{\text{in}}\mapsto\vec{\hat{v}}]\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\textsf{consume}(m,A_{i},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime\prime}\triangleq\{p(\vec{\hat{v}};\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\vec{x}_{\text{out}}))\}\cup\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.\mathsf{preds}}{\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{fold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{m}\mathit{ok}:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}[\mathsf{preds}:=\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime\prime}]}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Fold}\frac{|\vec{\mathsf{E}}|\neq|\vec{x}_{\text{in}}|}{\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{fold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{m}\mathit{abort}:\hat{\sigma}_{\mathit{err}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Fold-Err-ParamCount}\frac{|\vec{\mathsf{E}}|=|\vec{x}_{\text{in}}|\hskip 8.50012pt\llbracket\vec{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\lightning,\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}{\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{fold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{m}\mathit{abort}:\hat{\sigma}_{\mathit{err}}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}]}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Fold-Err-Eval}\frac{\llbracket\vec{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\pi})\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{\theta}\triangleq[\vec{x}_{\text{in}}\mapsto\vec{\hat{v}}]\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\forall i\in I.~\textsf{consume}(m,A_{i},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow\mathit{abort}}{\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{fold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{m}\mathit{abort}:\hat{\sigma}_{\mathit{err}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Fold-Err}\frac{\llbracket\vec{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\pi})\hskip 8.50012pt\textsf{consPred}(p,\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\vec{\hat{z}}\text{ fresh}\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{\theta}\triangleq[\vec{x}_{\text{in}}\mapsto\vec{\hat{v}},\vec{x}_{\text{out}}\mapsto\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}},\vec{x}_{i}\mapsto\vec{\hat{z}}]\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\textsf{produce}(A_{i},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}{\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{unfold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{m}\mathit{ok}:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Unfold}\frac{|\vec{\mathsf{E}}|\neq|\vec{x}_{\text{in}}|}{\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{unfold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{m}\mathit{abort}:\hat{\sigma}_{\mathit{err}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Unfold-Err-ParamCount}\frac{|\vec{\mathsf{E}}|=|\vec{x}_{\text{in}}|\hskip 8.50012pt\llbracket\vec{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\lightning,\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}{\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{unfold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{m}\mathit{abort}:\hat{\sigma}_{\mathit{err}}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}]}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Unfold-Err-Eval}\frac{|\vec{\mathsf{E}}|=|\vec{x}_{\text{in}}|\hskip 8.50012pt\llbracket\vec{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\pi})\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\textsf{consPred}(p,\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow\mathit{abort}}{\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{unfold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{m}\mathit{abort}:\hat{\sigma}_{\mathit{err}}}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Unfold-Err}

where
\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})
,
\hat{\sigma}_{\mathit{err}}\triangleq\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{sto}:=\hat{s}[\mathsf{err}\rightarrow``\mathit{\mathsf{fold/unfold}}"]]
, and
consPred
is defined as follows:

\frac{\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}};\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}})\}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\triangleq\hat{\pi}\wedge\vec{\hat{v}}=\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}}\hskip 17.00024pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}{\textsf{consPred}(p,\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}},\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{preds}:=\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])}\frac{\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in},i};\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out},i})\mid i\in I\}\uplus\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\hskip 8.50012ptp\notin\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}\wedge\neg(\bigwedge_{i\in I}\vec{\hat{v}}=\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in},i}))}{\textsf{consPred}(p,\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow abort}

B.6
Symbolic Testing

The following rules correspond to the symbolic semantics of the three commands added for symbolic testing. See App.
A.4
for more details.

\footnotesize\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 8.50012pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{v})}{((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi})),\mathtt{assume}(\mathsf{E})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land\hat{v}))}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Assume}\hfill\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\lightning,\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{v}_{\mathit{err}}\triangleq[``\mathit{\mathsf{ExprEval}}",\mathsf{str}(\mathsf{E})]}{((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi})),\mathtt{assume}(\mathsf{E})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{err}:((\hat{s}_{\mathit{err}},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}))}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Assume-Err}\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 8.50012pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{v})}{((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi})),\mathtt{assert}(\mathsf{E})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land\hat{v}))}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Assert-Ok}\hfill\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 8.50012pt\texttt{SAT}(\neg\hat{v})\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{v}_{\mathit{err}}\triangleq[``\mathit{\mathsf{Assert}}",\mathsf{str}(\mathsf{E})]}{((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi})),\mathtt{assert}(\mathsf{E})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:((\hat{s}_{\mathit{err}},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land\neg\hat{v}))}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Assert-Fail}\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\lightning,\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{v}_{\mathit{err}}\triangleq[``\mathit{\mathsf{ExprEval}}",\mathsf{str}(\mathsf{E}^{\prime})]}{((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi})),\mathtt{assert}(\mathsf{E})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{err}:((\hat{s}_{\mathit{err}},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}))}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Assert-Err}\hfill\frac{\hat{v}\text{ fresh}\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\pi}\land\hat{v}\in\mathsf{Val}}{((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi})),\mathsf{x}:=\textsf{sym}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{s}[\mathsf{x}\mapsto\hat{v}],\hat{h},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}))}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{Sym}

Appendix C
Core Symbolic Execution: Soundness

This appendix provides the relevant definitions, lemmas and proofs for the symbolic soundness theorems.

C.1
Further Definitions

Symbolic states
\hat{\sigma}
must satisfy certain well-formedness constraints, denoted by
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{\sigma})
. To define well-formedness, we need the following definition:
\hat{\pi}_{1}\models\hat{\pi}_{2}\triangleq\forall\varepsilon.~\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}_{1})={\sf true}\Rightarrow\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}_{2})={\sf true}
. Also note that symbolic values are countable; we enumerate them using the notation
\mathsf{SVal}=\{\hat{v}_{i}:i\in\mathsf{Nat}\}
. Now, we can define well-formedness: the store co-domain must not contain
\lightning
, given by
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s})
; the heap domain must contain disjoint addresses and its co-domain must not contain
\lightning
, given by
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h})
; and the path condition must be satisfiable and include all the symbolic variables of the state, ensuring that when the path condition can be interpreted so can the entire state:

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s})\triangleq{\sf codom}(\hat{s})\subseteq\mathsf{Val}\\
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h})\triangleq{\sf dom}(\hat{h})\subseteq\mathsf{Nat}\land{\sf codom}(\hat{h})\subseteq\mathsf{Val}\wedge(\forall\hat{v}_{i},\hat{v}_{j}\in{\sf dom}(\hat{h}).\;i\neq j\Rightarrow\hat{v}_{i}\neq\hat{v}_{j})\\
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}))\triangleq\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi})\land(\mathtt{sv}(\hat{s})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{h})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}))\land\hat{\pi}\models(\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s})\land\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h}))\end{array}

Given well-formedness, the definition of symbolic value interpretation is naturally extended to symbolic store, heap and state interpretation. The formal definitions are provided below:

\begin{array}[]{r@{~\defeq~}l@{~}l}\varepsilon(\hat{s})~\triangleq&\{\mathsf{x}\mapsto\varepsilon(\hat{s}(\mathsf{x}))\mid\mathsf{x}\in{\sf dom}(\hat{s})\},&\text{if~}\varepsilon(\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s}))={\sf true}\\
\varepsilon(\hat{h})~\triangleq&\{\varepsilon(\hat{v})\mapsto\varepsilon(\hat{h}(\hat{v}))\mid\hat{v}\in{\sf dom}(\hat{h})\},&\text{if~}\varepsilon(\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h}))={\sf true}\\
\varepsilon((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}))~\triangleq&(\varepsilon(\hat{s}),\varepsilon(\hat{h})),&\text{if~}\varepsilon(\pi)={\sf true}\land\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}))\end{array}

C.2
Evaluation Soundness

Lemma C.1

(Symbolic evaluation: Symbolic variables)
.

\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{w},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\implies\mathtt{sv}(\hat{w})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{s})

Lemma C.2

(Symbolic evaluation: Satisfiable outcome)
.

\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{w},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\implies\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

Lemma C.3

(Symbolic evaluation: Path condition)
.

\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{w},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\implies\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})={\sf true}\implies\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}

An aside: Note that it is, to be UX sound, important to not overreport errors: For example, for a languages with lazy conjunction semantics, evaluating the expression
\mathsf{c}\land(5~/~\mathsf{x}=4)
with path condition
\hat{\pi}
and a store
\hat{s}
with
\hat{s}(\mathsf{x})=\hat{\mathit{x}}
and
\hat{s}(\mathsf{c})=\hat{\mathit{c}}
, evaluation will branch into
\lightning
with
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{1}=\hat{\pi}\land\neg\hat{\mathit{c}}\land\hat{\mathit{x}}=0
and
\hat{\mathit{c}}\land(5~/~\hat{\mathit{x}}=4)
with
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{2}=\hat{\pi}\land\neg(\neg\hat{\mathit{c}}\land\hat{\mathit{x}}=0)
which simplifies to
\hat{\pi}\land(\hat{\mathit{c}}\lor\hat{\mathit{x}}\neq 0)
. Note that the simpler path condition
\hat{\pi}\land\hat{\mathit{x}}=0
for
\lightning
would overapproximate the error, and therefore not a possible path condition for a UX-sound symbolic expression evaluator. Here, however, we do not dwell into details.

Lemma C.4

(Symbolic evaluation: UX soundness)
.

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{w},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\land\varepsilon(\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s}))={\sf true}\land\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})={\sf true}\implies\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon(\hat{s})}=\varepsilon(\hat{w})

where
\hat{w}\in\mathsf{SVal}\cup\{\lightning\}
.

Lemma C.5

(Symbolic evaluation: OX soundness)
.

\displaystyle\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon(\hat{s})}=w\,\wedge\,\varepsilon(\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s}))={\sf true}\,\wedge\,\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}\implies

\displaystyle\exists\hat{\pi}^{\prime},\hat{w}.~\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{w},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\land\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})={\sf true}\,\wedge\,\varepsilon(\hat{w})=w

where
w\in\mathsf{Val}\cup\{\lightning\}
and
\hat{w}\in\mathsf{SVal}\cup\{\lightning\}
.

C.3
Execution Soundness

We give the proof of Thm.
1
– the OX and UX soundness of the engine – for a selection of the rules that are representative. All other cases work analogously.

OX Soundness

Mutate.

\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{1}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{1},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{h}(\hat{v}_{l})=\hat{v}_{m}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\triangleq(\hat{v}_{l}=\hat{v}_{1})\land\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{2}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{2},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{h}^{\prime}=\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{l}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}]}{(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),[\mathsf{E}_{1}]\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{E}_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{{Mutate}}

We assume a successful execution of the mutate command, i.e. some
s
,
h
,
n
and
v
such that

(H1)

\llbracket{\mathsf{E}_{1}}\rrbracket_{s}=n

(H2)

\llbracket{\mathsf{E}_{2}}\rrbracket_{s}=v

(H3)

h(n)\in\mathsf{Val}

(H4)

(s,h),[\mathsf{E}_{1}]\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{E}_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:(s,h[n\mapsto v])

and some
\varepsilon
,
\hat{s}
,
\hat{h}
and
\hat{\pi}
such that
(H5)

\varepsilon(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi})=(s,h)
, i.e.

(H5a)

\varepsilon(\hat{s})=s

(H5b)

\varepsilon(\hat{h})=h

(H5c)

\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})=\hat{\pi}

(H1), (H5) and Lemma
C.5
imply the existence of some
\hat{n}
and
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}
such that

(H6a)
\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{1}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{n},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

(H6b)

\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})={\sf true}

(H6c)

\varepsilon(\hat{n})=n

(H3), (H5b) and (H6c) imply the existence of some
\hat{m}\in\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{h})
such that

(H7a)

\varepsilon(\hat{n})=\varepsilon(\hat{m})

(H7b)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\triangleq(\hat{n}=\hat{m})~\land~\hat{\pi}^{\prime}

(This is merely a new definition.)

(H7c)

\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})={\sf true}
.
(This is a trivially implication of the above two, and it implies
\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
.)

(H7d)

\hat{h}(\hat{m})\neq\hat{w}
for some
\hat{w}\in\mathsf{SVal}
(i.e. it is not negative resource.)

(H2), (H5), (H7c) and Lemma
C.5
imply the existence of some
\hat{v}
and
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}
such that

(H8a)

\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{2}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}}\Downarrow(\hat{v},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})

(H8b)

\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})={\sf true}

(H8c)

\varepsilon(\hat{v})=v

We define
(H9a)

\hat{h}^{\prime}\triangleq\hat{h}[\hat{m}\mapsto\hat{v}]
.

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\hat{h}^{\prime})

\displaystyle=\varepsilon(\hat{h}[\hat{m}\mapsto\hat{v}])=\varepsilon(\hat{h})[\varepsilon(\hat{m})\mapsto\varepsilon(\hat{v})]=h[n\mapsto v]~\textbf{(H9b)}

where the last equality follows from (H5b), (H6c), (H7a) and (H8c).

(H5a), (H8b) and (H9b) imply
(H10)

\varepsilon(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})=(s,h[n\mapsto v])
.

(H6a), (H7b), (H7c), (H7d), (H8a) and (H9a) together with the symbolic execution rule for mutate yield
(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),[\mathsf{E}_{1}]\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{E}_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})
which, toegether with (H10) implies the desired result.

UX Soundness

Mutate.

Rule:

\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{1}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{1},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{h}(\hat{v}_{l})=\hat{v}_{m}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=(\hat{v}_{l}=\hat{v}_{1})\land\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{2}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{2},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{h}^{\prime}=\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{l}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}]}{(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),[\mathsf{E}_{1}]\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{E}_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{{Mutate}}

We assume

(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),[\mathsf{E}_{1}]\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{E}_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})

which yields

(H0)

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}_{\hat{\pi}}(\hat{s})
and
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}_{\hat{\pi}}(\hat{h})

(H1)

\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{1}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{1},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

(H2)

\hat{h}(\hat{v}_{l})=\hat{v}_{m}

(H3)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=(\hat{v}_{l}=\hat{v}_{1})\land\hat{\pi}^{\prime}

(H3)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=(\hat{v}_{l}=\hat{v}_{1})\land\hat{\pi}^{\prime}

(H4)

\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})

(H5)

\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{2}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{2},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})

(H6)

\hat{h}^{\prime}=\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{l}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}]

Now, let
\varepsilon(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})=(s,h^{\prime})
, for some
\varepsilon
,
s
and
h^{\prime}
. i.e. (given (H6))

(H7a)

\varepsilon(\hat{s})=s

(H7b)

\varepsilon(\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{l}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}])=h^{\prime}

(H7c)

\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})={\sf true}

(H1), (H3) and (H5) imply
(H8)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}\Rightarrow\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\hat{\pi}
and (H7c) yields
(H9)

\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})=\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})=\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})=\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}
.

(H0), (H2) and (H9) implies
(H10)

\varepsilon(\hat{v}_{l})\in\mathsf{Nat}
.

(H3) and (H9) imply
(H11a)

\varepsilon(\hat{v}_{l})=\varepsilon(\hat{v}_{1})
and we define
(H11b)

n=\varepsilon(\hat{v}_{l})=\varepsilon(\hat{v}_{1})\in\mathsf{Nat}
, given (H10).

We define
(H12a)

h=\varepsilon(\hat{h})
and (H11b) and (H12) then implies
(H12b)

h(n)\in\mathsf{Val}
.

(H6), (H7b), (H11a) and (H12a) implies
(H13)

h^{\prime}=h[n\mapsto\varepsilon(\hat{v}_{2})]
.

Given (H0), (H1), (H7a), (H9) and (H11a), Lemma
C.4
implies
(H14)

\llbracket{\mathsf{E}_{1}}\rrbracket_{s}=n
.

Given (H5), (H7a), (H9) and Lemma
C.4
yields
(H16)

\llbracket{\mathsf{E}_{2}}\rrbracket_{s}=\varepsilon^{\prime}(\hat{v}_{2})
.

Given (H12b), (H13), (H14) and (H16), the concrete semantics yields

(s,h),[\mathsf{E}_{1}]\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{E}_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}(s,h^{\prime})

and since (H7a), (H9) and (H13) implies
(s,h)=\varepsilon(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi})
, concluding the proof.

Free.

Rule:

\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{h}(\hat{v}_{l})=\hat{v}_{m}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=(\hat{v}_{l}=\hat{v})\land\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{h}^{\prime}=\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{l}\mapsto\varnothing]}{(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),\mathtt{free}(\mathsf{E})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{{Free}}

We assume

(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),\mathtt{free}(\mathsf{E})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{s},\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{l}\mapsto\varnothing],\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})

which yields

(H0)

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}_{\hat{\pi}}(\hat{s})
and
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}_{\hat{\pi}}(\hat{h})

(H1)

\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

(H2)

\hat{h}(\hat{v}_{l})=\hat{v}_{m}

(H3)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=(\hat{v}_{l}=\hat{v})\land\hat{\pi}^{\prime}

(H4)

\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt

Now, let
(s,h^{\prime})=\varepsilon(\hat{s},\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{l}\mapsto\hat{v}],\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
, for some
\varepsilon
,
s
and
h^{\prime}
. i.e.

(H6a)

\varepsilon(\hat{s})=s

(H6b)

\varepsilon(\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{l}\mapsto\varnothing])=h^{\prime}

(H6c)

\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})={\sf true}

(H1) and (H3) imply
(H7)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\Rightarrow\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\hat{\pi}
and through (H6c) we obtain
(H8)

\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})=\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})=\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}
.

We define
(H9)

h=\varepsilon(\hat{h})
.

Given (H0), (H1), (H8) amd (H6a), Lemma
C.4
yields
(H10a)

\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\varepsilon(\hat{\mathit{e}})
. Defining
n=\varepsilon(\hat{\mathit{e}})
, (H0), (H2), (H3), (H8) and (H10a) imply
(H10b)

n=\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\varepsilon(\hat{\mathit{e}})=\varepsilon(\hat{\mathit{e}}_{l})
.
With (H2) and (H9) we obtain
(H10c)

h(n)\in\mathsf{Val}
.

(H6b), (H9) and (H10b) yield
(H11)

h^{\prime}=h[n\mapsto\varnothing]
.

Given (H10b), (H10c) and (H11), the concrete semantics yields

(s,h),\mathtt{free}(\mathsf{E})\Downarrow{\gamma}(s,h^{\prime})

and (H6a), (H8) and (H9) imply
(s,h)=\varepsilon(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi})
, concluding the proof.

Seq.

Rule:

\frac{\hat{\sigma},C_{1}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},C_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}{\hat{\sigma},C_{1};C_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{{Seq}}

We assume

\hat{\sigma},C_{1};C_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}

which yields

(H1)

\hat{\sigma},C_{1}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}

(H2)

\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},C_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}

Let
\sigma^{\prime\prime}=\varepsilon(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime})
for some
\varepsilon
.
The inductive hypothesis and (H2) imply that for
\sigma^{\prime}=\varepsilon(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})
that we have
(H3)

\sigma^{\prime},\mathsf{C}_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime\prime}
.

The inductive hypothesis and (H1) imply that for
\sigma=\varepsilon(\hat{\sigma})
that we have
(H4)

\sigma,\mathsf{C}_{1}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:\sigma^{\prime}
.

Given (H3) and (H4), the concrete semantics imply

\sigma,\mathsf{C}_{1};\mathsf{C}_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime\prime}

As
\sigma=\varepsilon(\hat{\sigma})
, the proof is concluded.

Mutate-Err-Val-1.

Rule:

\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{1}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\lightning,\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{v}_{\mathit{err}}=[``\mathit{\mathsf{ExprEval}}",\mathsf{str}(\mathsf{E}_{1})]}{(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),[\mathsf{E}_{1}]\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{E}_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{err}:(\hat{s}_{\mathit{err}},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{{Mutate-Err-Val-1}}

We assume

(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),[\mathsf{E}_{1}]\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{E}_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{err}:(\hat{s}_{\mathit{err}},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

which yields

(H0)

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}_{\hat{\pi}}(\hat{s})

(H1)

\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
(from Lemma
C.2
)

(H2)

\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{1}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\lightning,\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

(H3)

\hat{v}_{\mathit{err}}=[``\mathit{\mathsf{ExprEval}}",\mathsf{str}(\mathsf{E}_{1})]

Now, let
(s^{\prime},h)=\varepsilon(\hat{s}_{\mathit{err}},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
, i.e.

(H4a)

\varepsilon(\hat{s}[\mathsf{err}\mapsto\hat{v}_{\mathit{err}}])=s^{\prime}

(H4b)

\varepsilon(\hat{h})=h

(H4c)

\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})={\sf true}

(H2) implies
(H5)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\hat{\pi}
, which implies with (H4c) that
(H6)

\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})=\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}
.

Define
(H7)

s=\varepsilon(\hat{s})
.

Given (H0), (H2), (H6) and (H7), Lemma
C.4
yields
(H8)

\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\lightning
.

As
\hat{v}_{\mathit{err}}
has no symbolic variables, we have
(H9)

v_{\mathit{err}}=\varepsilon(\hat{v}_{\mathit{err}})=\hat{v}_{\mathit{err}}
.

(H4a), (H7) and (H9) implies that
(H10)

s^{\prime}=s[\mathsf{err}\mapsto v_{\mathit{err}}]
.

Given (H10), (H8), (H9) and (H3), the operational semantics implies

(s,h),[\mathsf{E}_{1}]\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{E}_{2}\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{err}:(s^{\prime},h)

(H7), (H4b) and (H6) imply that
(s,h)=\varepsilon(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi})
, concluding the proof.

Appendix D
Compositional Symbolic Execution: Soundness

In this appendix we present the two soundness proofs of Thm.
3
for the consume-produce-based function call, predicate fold, and predicate unfold rules. We present a selection of proof cases, the other rules are either the same as in the proof of Thm.
1
, similar to the cases we present here, or simple.

D.1
Further Definitions for Function Specifications

Satisfaction Relation for Assertions.

The logical expression evaluation,
\llbracket{\mathit{E}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s}
, extends program expression evaluation to interpret the logical variables using
\theta
. The satisfaction relation for assertions, denoted by
\theta,\sigma\models P
, is standard:

\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{~}c@{~\ }l}\theta,(s,h)\models\\
\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{~}c@{~\ }l@{\qquad\qquad}l@{~}c@{~\ }l}\hskip 9.24994pt{{\pi}}&\hskip 1.42271pt\Leftrightarrow&\llbracket{{\pi}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s}={\sf true}\wedge h=\emptyset\hfil\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt&\mathsf{False}&\Leftrightarrow&\mbox{never}\\
\hskip 9.24994ptP_{1}\Rightarrow P_{2}&\hskip 1.42271pt\Leftrightarrow&\theta,(s,h)\models P_{1}\Rightarrow\theta,(s,h)\models P_{2}\hfil\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt&P_{1}\lor P_{2}&\Leftrightarrow&\theta,(s,h)\models P_{1}\lor\theta,(s,h)\models P_{2}\\
\hskip 9.24994pt\exists\mathit{x}\ldotp P&\hskip 1.42271pt\Leftrightarrow&\exists v\in\mathsf{Val}\ldotp\theta[\mathit{x}\mapsto v],(s,h)\models P\hfil\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt&{{\sf emp}}&\Leftrightarrow&{h=\emptyset}\\
\hskip 9.24994pt{\mathit{E}_{1}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{2}}&\hskip 1.42271pt\Leftrightarrow&h=\{\llbracket{\mathit{E}_{1}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s}\mapsto\llbracket{\mathit{E}_{2}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s}\}\hfil\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt&{\mathit{E}\mapsto\varnothing}&\Leftrightarrow&h=\{\llbracket{\mathit{E}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s}\mapsto\varnothing\}\\
\end{array}\\
\begin{array}[]{@{}l@{~}c@{~\ }l@{\qquad\qquad}l@{~}c@{~\ }l}\hskip 9.24994ptP_{1}\mathrel{\star}P_{2}&\Leftrightarrow&\exists h_{1},h_{2}\ldotp h=h_{1}\uplus h_{2}\land\theta,(s,h_{1})\models P_{1}\wedge\theta,(s,h_{2})\models P_{2}\hfil\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\\
\hskip 9.24994ptp(\vec{\mathit{E}_{1}};\vec{\mathit{E}_{2}})&\Leftrightarrow&\theta[\vec{x}_{\text{in}}\mapsto\llbracket{\vec{\mathit{E}_{1}}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s},\vec{x}_{\text{out}}\mapsto\llbracket{\vec{\mathit{E}_{2}}}\rrbracket_{\theta,s}],(s,h)\models P\text{ for }p(\vec{x}_{\text{in}};\vec{x}_{\text{out}})~\{P\}\in\mathsf{Preds}\hfil\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\end{array}\end{array}

Note that the meaning of Boolean assertions
\pi
is defined using the empty heap; the alternative is for them to be satisfied in any heap, definable here as
\pi\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{True}
. The meaning of the predicate assertion
p(\vec{\mathit{E}_{1}};\vec{\mathit{E}_{2}})
is defined in terms of its unfolding. An assertion
P
is
valid
, denoted
\models P
, iff
\forall\theta,s,h.~\theta,(s,h)\models P
.

OX and UX Specifications.

OX and UX specifications
have, respectively, the form

\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right]}\ {}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right]}

for precondition
P
, successful postcondition
Q_{\mathit{ok}}
and faulting
postcondition
Q_{\mathit{err}}
. To denote either type of specification, we write

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}\ {\!}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}
.
If execution only succeeds or only faults, we omit the
other post-condition instead of putting an unsatisfiable assertion.
Given implementation context
\gamma
, we define
\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}\ {\mathsf{C}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}
, denoting that specification

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}\ {\!}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}
of command
\mathsf{C}
is

\gamma
-valid, by

\begin{array}[]{l}\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {\mathsf{C}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\}}\triangleq\forall\theta,s,h,o,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}.\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\theta,(s,h)\models P\land(s,h),\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\implies(o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\theta,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\models Q_{o})\\[2.84526pt]
\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right]}\ {\mathsf{C}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right]}\triangleq\forall\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime},o.\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\theta,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\models Q_{o}\implies(\exists s,h\ldotp~\theta,(s,h)\models P~\land~(s,h),\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}))\end{array}

Internal and External Function Specifications.

Function specifications comprise
external
specifications, which describe the function interface toward its callers, and
internal
specifications, satisfied by the function body. An external specification has the form

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}\ {}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}

where:

•

\vec{\mathsf{x}}
/
\vec{x}
are distinct program/logical variables,

\mathtt{pv}(P)=\emptyset
, and
P
has no existentials;

•

Q_{\mathit{ok}}/Q_{\mathit{err}}
are either unsatisfiable or are of the form
\exists\vec{y}.~Q
, with

Q
having no existential quantification and
\mathtt{pv}(Q)=\{\mathsf{ret}/\mathsf{err}\}
.

The constraints on the program variables in external specifications
are well-known from OX logics and follow the usual scoping of the
parameters and local variables of functions. No other program variables can be present in the two post-conditions due to variable scope being limited to the function body.

The relationship between external and internal UX specifications
is more complex than in OX reasoning. In particular, while the
internal UX pre-condition extends the
external by simply instantiating the locals to null,
the transition from internal to external post-condition must ensure
that:
(P1)
no information is lost (for UX soundness); and
(P2)
no locals leak into
the calling context (given variable scoping). To capture this, as is done in the work on ESL
[
19
]
, we define an

internalisation
function,
\mathit{Int}_{\gamma,f}^{\textsf{UX}}
, which takes an
implementation context
\gamma
, a function

f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\,\{\,\mathsf{C};\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{E}\,\}\in\gamma
, and a UX specification
and returns the set of all of its internal specifications:

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathit{Int}_{\gamma,f}^{\textsf{UX}}({\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathit{\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P}\end{array}\right]}\ {\!}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right]})\triangleq\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\{~{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\mathit{\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P}\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}\right]}\ {\!}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right]}~{:}\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt~\models(Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\Rightarrow\mathsf{E}\in\mathsf{Val}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{True}),\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt~\models(Q_{\mathit{ok}}\Rightarrow\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\{\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}\}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}\{\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}\}),\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt~\models(Q_{\mathit{err}}\Rightarrow\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\{\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}\})~\}\end{array}

where

\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{C})\backslash\{\vec{\mathsf{x}}\}
,
\vec{\mathsf{p}}=\{\vec{\mathsf{x}}\}\uplus\{\vec{\mathsf{z}}\}
, and the logical variables
\vec{p}
are fresh w.r.t.
Q_{\mathit{ok}}
and
Q_{\mathit{err}}
.
In particular, the external post-condition must be equal to or stronger than the internal one (ensuring P1) in which the parameters and local variables have been replaced by fresh existentially quantified logical variables (ensuring P2).
OX specification internalisation, denoted by

\mathit{Int}_{\gamma,f}^{\textsf{OX}}
, is defined analogously, with the two latter
implications reversed: e.g.,

\models(Q_{\mathit{ok}}\Leftarrow\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}\{\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}\}
).
Explicit OX internalisation is not strictly necessary in SL, however, as information about program variables can be forgotten in internal post-conditions using forward consequence.

The set of external specifications is denoted by
\mathcal{E\mkern-1.0muS}\mkern-1.0mupec
. A function specification context (also: specification context),
\Gamma\in\mathsf{Fid}\rightharpoonup_{\mathit{fin}}\mathcal{P}(\mathcal{E\mkern-1.0muS}\mkern-1.0mupec)
, is a finite partial function from function identifiers to a finite set of external specifications.

Function Environments.

A function environment,
(\gamma,\Gamma)
, is a pair comprising an implementation context
\gamma
and a specification context
\Gamma
, where specification contexts map function identifiers to sets of their external specifications. A function environment
(\gamma,\Gamma)
is valid, written
\models(\gamma,\Gamma)
, iff every function in
\Gamma
has an implementation in
\gamma
and for every external specification in
\Gamma
there exists a corresponding internal specification valid under
\gamma
. Lastly, a function specification is valid in specification context
\Gamma
, denoted by
\Gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}\ {f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}
iff for all
\gamma
such that
\models(\gamma,\Gamma)
it holds that
\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}\ {f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\langle\!\!\left\langle err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\rangle\!\!\right\rangle}
.

D.2
Further Definitions for Extended Symbolic States

From now on, instead of denoting extended symbolic states as
(\hat{s},\hat{H},\hat{\pi})
we will expose the contents of
\hat{H}
and write
extended symbolic states in full, as
(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})
.

Well-formedness of extended states is defined by:

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}))\triangleq\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}))\land\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\mathcal{P}})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi})

In the main text, we use

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}((\hat{h}_{1},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{1}))\triangleq\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h}_{1})\text{ and }(\hat{h}_{1},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{1})\cup(\hat{h}_{2},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{2})\triangleq(\hat{h}_{1}\uplus\hat{h}_{2},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{1}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{2}).

and we refer to the definition of well-formed symbolic heaps
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h}_{1})
in §
C.1
.

Well-formed of a symbolic substitution
\hat{\theta}
, used in the consume and produce functions, with respect to
\hat{\pi}
is defined to be

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{\theta},\hat{\pi})\triangleq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi})\land\hat{\pi}\models\mathop{\mathrm{codom}}(\hat{\theta})\subseteq\mathsf{Val}.

As some symbolic predicates can be satisfied by multiple states, symbolic interpretations become a relation,
\models
, similar to the satisfaction relation for assertions:

\varepsilon,(s,h)\models(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})\Leftrightarrow\exists h_{1},h_{2}.~h=h_{1}\uplus h_{2}\land\varepsilon((\hat{s},\hat{h}_{1},\hat{\pi}))=(s,h_{1})\wedge\varepsilon,(s,h_{2})\models\hat{\mathcal{P}}

where
\varepsilon,\sigma\models\hat{\mathcal{P}}
denotes
\varepsilon,\sigma\models\lx@scalerel@obj{\mathrel{\star}}\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{1};\vec{\hat{v}}_{2})~|~p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{1};\vec{\hat{v}}_{2})\in\hat{\mathcal{P}}\}
.

Example D.1

(Interpretation of symbolic states)
.

Let
(\emptyset,(\{1\mapsto 5\},\{\mathsf{list}(\hat{\mathit{x}};[\hat{\mathit{x}}],[15])\}),\hat{\mathit{x}}\in\mathsf{Val})
be a symbolic state,
\varepsilon
be an interpretation such that
\varepsilon(\hat{\mathit{x}})=2
. In addition, consider the concrete state
(\emptyset,\{1\mapsto 5,2\mapsto 15,3\mapsto{\sf null}\})
. We can split the concrete heap in two disjoint parts such that
\varepsilon(\emptyset,\{1\mapsto 5\},\hat{\mathit{x}}\in\mathsf{Val})=(\emptyset,\{1\mapsto 5\})
and
\varepsilon,(\emptyset,\{2\mapsto 15,3\mapsto{\sf null}\}\models\mathsf{list}(\hat{\mathit{x}};[\hat{\mathit{x}}],[15])
hold. By definition the latter is equivalent to
\varepsilon,(\emptyset,\{2\mapsto 15,3\mapsto{\sf null}\}\models P_{list}
, where
P_{list}
is the predicate’s body as defined in §
5.1
.

D.3
Additional Lemmas

The following lemmas are convenient in our proofs and follows easily from the fact that our concrete language is compositional (Lem.
A.1
) and from the definition of symbolic state satisfaction (respectively):

Lemma D.2

(Specification Validity with Explicit Frame)
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right]}\ {\mathsf{C}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right]}\triangleq\\
\hskip 9.24994pt(\forall\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime},h_{f},o.~\theta,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\models Q_{o}\land h_{f}~\sharp~h^{\prime}\implies\\
\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt(\exists s,h\ldotp~\theta,(s,h)\models P~\land~(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f})))\\
\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {\mathsf{C}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\}}\triangleq\\
\hskip 9.24994pt(\forall\theta,s,h,h_{f},o,s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime}.~\theta,(s,h)\models P\land(s,h\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime\prime})\implies\\
\hskip 18.49988pt(o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\exists h^{\prime}.~h^{\prime\prime}=h^{\prime}\uplus h_{f}\land\theta,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\models Q_{o}))\\
\end{array}

Lemma D.3

(State decomposability)
.

\begin{array}[]{r@{\ }l}&(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})=(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{a},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{a},\hat{\pi})\cdot(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{b},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{b},\hat{\pi})\\
\land&\varepsilon,\sigma\models(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})\\
\land&\varepsilon,\sigma_{a}\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{a},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{a},\hat{\pi})\\
\implies&\exists\sigma_{b}.\ \varepsilon,\sigma_{b}\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{b},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{b},\hat{\pi})\land\sigma=\sigma_{a}\cdot\sigma_{b}\end{array}

D.4
Underapproximate Soundness Proof

The formal definition of strictly exact assertions
[
32
, p. 149]
, which are assertions that are satisfiable by at most one heap, is, if
\theta,(s,h)\models P
and
\theta,(s,h^{\prime})\models P
, then
h^{\prime}=h
. We call a predicate strictly exact iff its body is strictly exact.

UX soundness function call case.

Here, we present the proof of for
\mathit{ok}
-outcome for function calls, that is, when
\mathsf{C}=\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{E}})
.

We have a symbolic step as follows:

\small(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}),\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{UX}}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{s}[\mathsf{y}\mapsto\hat{\mathit{r}}],\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})\lx@proof@logical@and\begin{array}[]{r@{\hspace{.5cm}}l}\end{array}\llbracket\vec{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}){\\
}\mbox{{(H2)}}{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\end{array}\right]}\ {f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right]}\in\Gamma(f)|_{\textsf{UX}}{\\
}\mbox{{(H3)}}\hat{\theta}=\{\vec{\hat{v}}/\vec{x}\}{\\
}\mbox{{(H4)}}\textsf{consume}(\textsf{UX},P,\hat{\theta},(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}))\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})){\\
}\mbox{{(H5)}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}=\exists\vec{y}.~Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}{\\
}\mbox{{(H6)}}\vec{\hat{z}}\text{ fresh}{\\
}\mbox{{(H7)}}\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\theta}{{}^{\prime}}\uplus\{\vec{\hat{z}}/\vec{y}\}{\\
}\mbox{{(H8)}}r,\hat{r}\text{ fresh}{\\
}\mbox{{(H9)}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime}=Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\{r/\mathsf{ret}\}\text{~and~}\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\uplus\{\hat{r}/r\}{\\
}\mbox{{(H10)}}\textsf{produce}(Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime},\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime},(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}))\rightsquigarrow(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})\lx@close@alignment\end{equation*}where$\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})$andthefinalsymbolicstateis\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=(\hat{s}[\mathsf{y}\mapsto\hat{\mathit{r}}],\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})and,bydefinitionof\textsf{produce},{(H10a)}$\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\wedge\hat{\pi}_{q}$,forsome\hat{\pi}_{q},suchthat\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}).Ourtaskisnowtoconstructananalogousconcretestep.\par\par\par Let\varepsilon and\sigma^{\prime}=(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})besuchthat\varepsilon,\sigma^{\prime}\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.Bydefinitionof\models:\par$$\begin{aligned} \hskip 18.49988pt\varepsilon,\sigma^{\prime}\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow~&\exists h_{1}^{\prime},h_{2}^{\prime}.~h=h_{1}^{\prime}\uplus h_{2}^{\prime}\land\varepsilon(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.\mathsf{sto})=s^{\prime}\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{h}^{\prime})=h_{1}^{\prime}\wedge\\
&\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})={\sf true}~\wedge\varepsilon,(s^{\prime},h_{2}^{\prime})\models\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\end{aligned}$$\par\par\par\par Fromsoundnessof\textsf{consume}and\textsf{produce}(Prop.~\ref{prop:soundness}),wehave:$$\begin{array}[]{l@{\hspace{1cm}}l}\mbox{{(H4a)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{p}\uplus\hat{h}_{f},\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&\mbox{{(H4b)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{p}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f}\\

\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\description@item@[\hyp{10g}]Define $\hat{\sigma}_{f}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})$.
\end{description}\par Similarly,itfollowsthatthereexistsaconcretestate\sigma_{q}^{\prime}=(s^{\prime},h_{q})suchthat\begin{description}\description@item@[\hyp{11}]~$\varepsilon,\sigma_{q}^{\prime}\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime}},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})$
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\description@item@[\hyp{11a}]~$h_{q}=h_{q}^{\prime}\uplus h_{q}^{\prime\prime}$.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\description@item@[\hyp{11b}]~$\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}),\sigma_{q}^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime}$, by soundness of {produce} (Prop.~\ref{prop:soundness}).
Thus,
$\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}),\sigma_{q}^{\prime}\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\{r/\mathsf{ret}\}$, by definition.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\description@item@[\hyp{11c}]$\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}),(\emptyset,h_{q})\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\{r/\mathsf{ret}\}$,
since there are no program variables in
$Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\{r/\mathsf{ret}\}$. \end{description}\par Noticethat{(H10e)}{h_{f}\mathbin{\sharp}h_{q}},{(H10f)},{(H11c)}andcompletenessof\textsf{produce}(Prop.~\ref{prop:prod-compl}),impliesthatthereexists\hat{\sigma}_{Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime}}suchthat\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\hat{\sigma}_{f}\cdot\hat{\sigma}_{Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime}}andthat{(H11d)}~\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{q})\models\hat{\sigma}_{Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime}}.Take\hat{\sigma}_{Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime}}=(\emptyset,\hat{h}_{Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime}},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}).\par\par Bythehypothesis$\models(\gamma,\Gamma)$,onehas,forsome\mathsf{C}^{\prime}and\mathsf{E}^{\prime},{(H12)}f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\{\mathsf{C}^{\prime};\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{E}^{\prime}\}\in\gamma andavalidUXquadruple$$\mbox{{(H12a)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\star P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathsf{null}\end{array}\right]}\ {\mathsf{C}^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{*}\end{array}\right]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{*}\end{array}\right]}$$suchthat\par\begin{description}\description@item@[\hyp{12b}]$\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{*}\implies\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Val}$
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\description@item@[\hyp{12c}]$\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}\implies\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{*}\{\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}\}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\{\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}\}~\text{and}$
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\description@item@[\hyp{12d}]$\models Q_{\mathit{err}}\implies\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}^{*}\{\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}\}$
\end{description}where\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{C}^{\prime})\backslash\{\vec{\mathsf{x}}\},\vec{\mathsf{p}}=\{\vec{\mathsf{x}},\vec{\mathsf{z}}\},andthelogicalvariables\vec{p}arefreshwithrespecttoQ_{\mathit{ok}}andQ_{\mathit{err}}.\par Now,{(H5)}$Q_{\mathit{ok}}=\exists\vec{y}.Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}$and{(H11c)}$\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}),(\emptyset,h_{q})\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\{r/\mathsf{ret}\}$entails\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}),(\emptyset,h_{q})\models~Q_{\mathit{ok}}\{r/\mathsf{ret}\}.Byproperty{(H12c)}onehas$\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}),(\emptyset,h_{q})\models\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{*}\{\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}\}\mathrel{\star}r=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\{\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}\}$.Thus,thereexists\vec{w}\in\mathsf{Val}suchthat$$\mbox{{(H13)}}\hskip 18.49988pt\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}),(\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{p}}\mapsto\vec{w}],h_{q})\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{*}\mathrel{\star}r=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}.$$Define\begin{description}\description@item@[\hyp{13a}]$s_{q}=\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{p}}\mapsto\vec{w}]$;
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\description@item@[\hyp{13b}]$\theta_{0}=\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime})$.
\end{description}Byhypothesis{(H9)}$\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\uplus\{r\mapsto\hat{\mathit{r}}\}$weobtain{(H13d)}\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{\theta_{0},s_{q}}=\theta_{0}(r)=\varepsilon(\hat{\mathit{r}}).\par ValidityoftheUXquadruple{(H12a)}$\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\star P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathsf{null}\end{array}\right]}\ {\mathsf{C}^{\prime}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{*}\end{array}\right]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{*}\end{array}\right]}$togetherwithLem.~\ref{lem:valid-with-frame},{(H13)},{(H10a)},and{(H10e)}{h_{f}\mathbin{\sharp}h_{q}}implythat$$\mbox{{(H14)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\exists s_{p},h_{p}\ldotp~\theta_{0},(s_{p},h_{p})\models\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathsf{null}~\land~(s_{p},h_{p}\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{C}^{\prime}\Downarrow_{\gamma}ok:(s_{q},h_{q}\uplus h_{f})$$Using{(H13b)},weobtain\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}),(s_{p},h_{p})\models P.SincePdoesnothaveanyprogramvariable,wecanconclude\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}),(\emptyset,h_{p})\models P.Fromthefactthatr,\hat{\mathit{r}}arefresh,andwecanassumew.l.o.g.that\vec{y}donotoccurinP;thus,wecanconcludethat{(H15)}\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),(\emptyset,h_{p})\models P.Inparticular,wehave:\begin{description}\description@item@[\hyp{15a}]$\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),(s_{p},h_{p})\models\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathsf{null}$
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\description@item@[\hyp{15b}]$(s_{p},h_{p}\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{C}^{\prime}\Downarrow_{\gamma}ok:(s_{q},h_{q}\uplus h_{f})$,
\end{description}where\hat{s}_{p}=\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\mapsto\vec{x}][\vec{\mathsf{z}}\mapsto\mathsf{null}]ands_{p}=\varepsilon(\hat{s}_{p})=\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\mapsto\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\vec{x}))][\vec{\mathsf{z}}\mapsto\mathsf{null}](becauseanyotherprogramvariablescannotaffectexecution),thatis,{(H15c)}s_{p}=[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\mapsto\vec{v},\vec{\mathsf{z}}\mapsto\mathsf{null}],where\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\vec{x}))=\varepsilon(\vec{\hat{v}})=\vec{v}.\par Now,wedefine\hat{\sigma}_{P}=(\emptyset,\hat{h}_{p},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{p},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}).From{(H4)},{(H15)},{(H10f)}and{(H14)}{h_{f}\mathbin{\sharp}h_{p}},wehavealltheconditionsforthecompletenessofUX\textsf{consume}(Prop.~\ref{prop:mac-ux-comp})whichimplies\varepsilon,(s,h_{f}\uplus h_{p})\models\hat{\sigma}\wedge\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{p})\models\hat{\sigma}_{P}.Since$\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})={\sf true}$,UXsoundnessofevaluation(Thm.~\ref{thm:ux-sound-eval}),and{(H1)},thefollowingholds:{(H16)}$\llbracket{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{s}=\vec{v}$,fors=\varepsilon(\hat{s}).\par From{(H12)},{(H13d)},{(H15b)},{(H15c)}and{(H16)}weobtainallcomponentsrequiredforourconcretefunctioncall:$$({s},{h_{p}\uplus h_{f}}),\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\gamma}({s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}]},{h_{q}\uplus h_{f}})\begin{array}[]{c}f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\,\{\,\mathsf{C}^{\prime};\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{E}^{\prime}\,\}\in\gamma\\
\llbracket{\vec{\mathsf{E}}}\rrbracket_{s}=\vec{v}\hskip 9.24994pt\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{C}^{\prime})\setminus\{\vec{\mathsf{x}}\}=\{\vec{\mathsf{z}}\}\\
s_{p}=\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\rightarrow\vec{v}][\vec{\mathsf{z}}\rightarrow\mathtt{null}]\\
(s_{p},h_{p}\uplus h_{f}),\mathsf{C}^{\prime}\Downarrow_{\gamma}({s_{q}},{h_{q}\uplus h_{f}})\hskip 9.24994pt\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{s_{q}}=v^{\prime}\end{array}$$andtheresultsfollows.\par

Folding Predicates Case:
\mathsf{C}=\mathtt{fold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})
.

We want to prove the following:

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)\land\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{fold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{UX}}o:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\land\varepsilon,\sigma^{\prime}\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\implies\exists\sigma.~\varepsilon,\sigma\models\hat{\sigma}\land\sigma,\mathtt{fold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}

Since
\sigma,\mathtt{fold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:\sigma
, we take
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}
. I.e., we must show
\varepsilon,\sigma^{\prime}\models\hat{\sigma}
.

The rule for folding predicates is:

\frac{\mbox{ {(H1)}}\hskip 9.24994ptp(\vec{x}_{\text{in}})(\vec{x}_{\text{out}})~\{\bigvee_{i}(\exists\vec{x}_{i}.~A_{i})\}\in\mathsf{Preds}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{ {(H2)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\llbracket\vec{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\pi})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{ {(H3)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}\triangleq[\vec{x}_{\text{in}}\mapsto\vec{\hat{v}}]\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{ {(H4)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consume}(m,A_{i},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}_{i})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{ {(H5)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\triangleq\{p(\vec{\hat{v}})(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\vec{x}_{\text{out}}))\}\cup\hat{\sigma}_{i}.\mathsf{preds}}{\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{fold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{m}\mathit{ok}:\hat{\sigma}_{i}[\mathsf{preds}:=\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}]}

where
\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})
and

(H6)

\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\hat{\sigma}_{i}[\mathsf{preds}:=\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}]
.

Let
\varepsilon
and
\sigma^{\prime}=(s,h^{\prime})
be such that
\varepsilon,\sigma^{\prime}\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}
. Then, by definition of
\models
:

\displaystyle\mbox{{(H7)}}\hskip 18.49988pt\varepsilon,\sigma^{\prime}\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\exists h_{1}^{\prime},h_{2}^{\prime}.~h=h_{1}^{\prime}\uplus h_{2}^{\prime}\land\varepsilon(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.\mathsf{sto})=s\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.\mathsf{heap})=h_{1}^{\prime}\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.\mathsf{pc})={\sf true}~\wedge

\displaystyle\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{2}^{\prime})\models\lx@scalerel@obj{\mathrel{\star}}\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{1})(\vec{\hat{v}}_{2})~|~p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{1})(\vec{\hat{v}}_{2})\in\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\}

From
\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{2}^{\prime})\models\lx@scalerel@obj{\mathrel{\star}}\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{1})(\vec{\hat{v}}_{2})~|~p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{1})(\vec{\hat{v}}_{2})\in\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\exists h_{21}^{\prime},h_{22}^{\prime}.~h_{2}^{\prime}=h_{21}^{\prime}\uplus h_{22}^{\prime}~\wedge

\displaystyle\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{21}^{\prime})\models p(\vec{\hat{v}})(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\vec{x}_{\text{out}}))

\displaystyle\wedge\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{22}^{\prime})\models\lx@scalerel@obj{\mathrel{\star}}\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{1})(\vec{\hat{v}}_{2})~|~p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{1})(\vec{\hat{v}}_{2})\in\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\}

Thus,

(H8)

\varepsilon,(s,h_{1}^{\prime}\uplus h_{22}^{\prime})\models\hat{\sigma}_{i}
.

Moreover, by definition,
\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{21}^{\prime})\models p(\vec{\hat{v}})(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\vec{x}_{\text{out}}))\Leftrightarrow\theta,(\emptyset,h_{21}^{\prime})\models\bigvee_{j}(\exists\vec{x}_{j}.~A_{j})
where
\theta=\{\varepsilon(\vec{\hat{v}})/\vec{x}_{\text{in}},\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\vec{x}_{\text{out}}))/\vec{x}_{\text{out}}\}
.

By soundness of
consume
(Prop.
4
), we have
\hat{h}_{f}
and
\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f}
such that:

\begin{array}[]{l@{\hspace{1cm}}l}\mbox{{(H4a)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{A_{i}}\uplus\hat{h}_{f},\hfil\hskip 28.45274pt&\mbox{{(H4b)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{A_{i}}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f}\end{array}

which implies that
(H9)

\hat{\sigma}_{i}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
, where
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\pi}_{i}
(Prop.
2
), for some
\hat{\pi}_{i}
, and
(H10)

\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{A_{i}},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{A_{i}},\hat{\pi})\cdot(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\hat{\pi})
.

We have
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})={\sf true}\Rightarrow\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}
, and hence, there exist
h
such that
\varepsilon,(s,h)\models\hat{\sigma}
. In consequence together with
(H10)
, there exist
h_{A_{i}}
such that
\varepsilon,(s,h_{A_{i}})\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{A_{i}},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{A_{i}},\hat{\pi})
. It follows from soundness of
consume
that
\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),(\emptyset,h_{A_{i}})\models A_{i}
. This in turn implies
\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),(\emptyset,h_{A_{i}})\models\exists\vec{x}_{i}.~A_{i}
, which in turn together with Prop.
3
implies
\theta,(\emptyset,h_{A_{i}})\models\exists\vec{x}_{i}.~A_{i}
, which in turn implies
\theta,(\emptyset,h_{A_{i}})\models\bigvee_{j}\exists\vec{x}_{j}.~A_{j}
. Since for UX folding we require the predicate to be strictly exact, it follows that
h_{A_{i}}=h^{\prime}_{21}
.

(H8)
and
(H9)
implies
\varepsilon,(s,h_{1}^{\prime}\uplus h_{22}^{\prime})\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\hat{\pi})
.
(H10)
now gives
\varepsilon,(s,h^{\prime}_{21}\uplus(h_{1}^{\prime}\uplus h_{22}^{\prime}))\models\hat{\sigma}
.

Unfolding Predicates Case:
\mathsf{C}=\mathtt{unfold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})
.

We want to prove the following:

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)\land\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{unfold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{UX}}o:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\land\varepsilon,\sigma^{\prime}\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\implies\exists\sigma.~\varepsilon,\sigma\models\hat{\sigma}\land\sigma,\mathtt{unfold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}

Since
\sigma,\mathtt{unfold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\gamma}\mathit{ok}:\sigma
, we take
\sigma=\sigma^{\prime}
. We must now show
\varepsilon,\sigma^{\prime}\models\hat{\sigma}
.

The symbolic rule for unfolding a predicate is:

\frac{\mbox{ {(H1)}}\hskip 9.24994ptp(\vec{x}_{\text{in}})(\vec{x}_{\text{out}})~\{\bigvee_{i}(\exists\vec{x}_{i}.~A_{i})\}\in\mathsf{Preds}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{ {(H2)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\llbracket\vec{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\pi})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{ {(H3)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consPred}(p,\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}},\hat{\sigma}_{p})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{ {(H4)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\vec{\hat{z}}\text{ fresh}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{ {(H5)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}\triangleq[\vec{x}_{\text{in}}\mapsto\vec{\hat{v}},\vec{x}_{\text{out}}\mapsto\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}},\vec{x}_{i}\mapsto\vec{\hat{z}}]\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mbox{ {(H6)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{produce}(A_{i},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma}_{p})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}_{p}^{\prime}}{\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{unfold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{m}\mathit{ok}:\hat{\sigma}_{p}^{\prime}}

where
\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})
,
\hat{\sigma}_{p}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{p},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{p},\hat{\pi}_{p})
, and

(H8)

\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\hat{\sigma}_{p}^{\prime}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime}_{p},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}_{p},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{p})
.

From soundness of
produce
(Prop.
4
), we obtain that there exist
\hat{h}_{A_{i}}
and
\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{A_{i}}
such that
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}_{p}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{p},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{p},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{p})\cdot(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{A_{i}},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{A_{i}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{p})
.

We have
\varepsilon,\sigma^{\prime}\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}_{p}
. Say
\sigma^{\prime}=(s,h^{\prime})
. There exist
h_{A_{i}}
and
h^{\prime}_{p}
such that
h^{\prime}=h_{A_{i}}\uplus h_{p}
,
\varepsilon,(s,h_{p})\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{p},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{p},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{p})
, and
\varepsilon,(s,h_{A_{i}})\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{A_{i}},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{A_{i}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}_{p})
. From this it follows that
\varepsilon,(s,h_{p})\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{p},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{p},\hat{\pi}_{p})
.

From the definition of
consPred
it follows that there exist
\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}}
such that
\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}})(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}})\}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{p}
and
\hat{\pi}_{p}=(\hat{\pi}\wedge\vec{\hat{v}}=\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}})
.

Note that we have from soundness of
produce
that
\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),(\emptyset,h_{A_{i}})\models A_{i}
, and hence
\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),(\emptyset,h_{A_{i}})\models p(\vec{x}_{\text{in}})(\vec{x}_{\text{out}})
, and hence
\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{A_{i}})\models p(\hat{\theta}(\vec{x}_{\text{in}}))(\hat{\theta}(\vec{x}_{\text{out}}))
, and hence
\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{A_{i}})\models p(\vec{\hat{v}})(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}})
, and hence
\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{A_{i}})\models p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}})(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}})
.

Since
h_{A_{i}}
models
p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}})(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}})
and
h^{\prime}_{p}
models
\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{p}
, the result now follows.

D.5
Overapproximate Soundness Proof

We take, as in the previous section, successful function call as our illustrative example for the proof of Thm.
3
.

OX soundness function call case.

We have

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\hat{\sigma},\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{OX}}\hat{\Sigma}^{\prime}

(H3)

\mathit{abort}\not\in\hat{\Sigma}^{\prime}

(H4)

\varepsilon,\sigma\models\hat{\sigma}\land\sigma,\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}

We start out with the following step in the concrete semantics
(H5)
:

({s},{h}),\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\gamma}({s[\mathsf{y}\rightarrow v^{\prime}]},{h^{\prime}})\begin{array}[]{c}f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\,\{\,\mathsf{C};\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{E}^{\prime}\,\}\in\gamma\\
\llbracket{\vec{\mathsf{E}}}\rrbracket_{s}=\vec{v}\hskip 9.24994pt\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{C})\setminus\{\vec{\mathsf{x}}\}=\vec{\mathsf{z}}\\
s_{p}=\emptyset[\vec{\mathsf{x}}\rightarrow\vec{v}][\vec{\mathsf{z}}\rightarrow\mathtt{null}]\\
(s_{p},h),\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}({s_{q}},{h^{\prime}})\hskip 9.24994pt\llbracket{\mathsf{E}^{\prime}}\rrbracket_{s_{q}}=v^{\prime}\end{array}

and must now construct an analogous step in the symbolic semantics.

That is, a step using the following rule:

\small(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),\mathsf{y}\mathtt{\ :=\ }f(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{OX}}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{s}[\mathsf{y}\mapsto\hat{\mathit{r}}],\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})\begin{array}[]{l}\llbracket\vec{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\\
{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\}}\in\Gamma(f)|_{\textsf{OX}}\\
\hat{\theta}=\{\vec{\hat{v}}/\vec{x}\}\\
\textsf{consume}(\textsf{OX},P,\hat{\theta},(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}))\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}))\\
Q_{\mathit{ok}}=\exists\vec{y}.~Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\\
\vec{\hat{z}}\text{ fresh}\\
\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\theta}{{}^{\prime}}\uplus\{\vec{\hat{z}}/\vec{y}\}\\
r,\hat{r}\text{ fresh}\\
Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime}=Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\{r/\mathsf{ret}\}\text{~and~}\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime}\uplus\{\hat{r}/r\}\\
\textsf{produce}(Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime},\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime\prime},(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}))\rightsquigarrow(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})\end{array}

Let
\sigma=(s,h),\sigma^{\prime}=(s[\mathsf{y}\mapsto v^{\prime}],h^{\prime})
, and
\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})
such that
(H6)

\varepsilon,\sigma\models\hat{\sigma}
(from
(H4)
). Note that in particular we have
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}
.

Say
f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\{\mathsf{C};\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{E}^{\prime}\}\in\gamma
. From
(H1)
, the definition of OX valid environments, and the definition of OX specification internalisation one has

•

(H1a)

\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\star P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathsf{null}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\mathsf{C}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{*}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{*}\end{array}\right\}}

•

(H1b)

\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{*}\implies\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Val}

•

(H1c)

\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}\Longleftarrow\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{*}\{\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}\}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}^{\prime}\{\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}\}~\text{and}

•

(H1d)

\models Q_{\mathit{err}}\Longleftarrow\exists\vec{p}\ldotp Q_{\mathit{err}}^{*}\{\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}\}

where
\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{C})\backslash\{\vec{\mathsf{x}}\}
,

, and the logical variables

are fresh with respect to
Q_{\mathit{ok}}
and

.

Since
\llbracket{\vec{\mathsf{E}}}\rrbracket_{s}=\vec{v}
,

and
\hat{v}

~\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

By

5
) and
(H3)

;

Moreover, from soundness it also follows that

Folding Predicates Case:
\mathsf{C}=\mathtt{fold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})
.

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\hat{\sigma},\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{OX}}\hat{\Sigma}^{\prime}

(H3)

\mathit{abort}\not\in\hat{\Sigma}^{\prime}

\varepsilon,\sigma\models\hat{\sigma}\land\sigma,\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}

To prove
:

\exists\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime}\geq\varepsilon.~(o,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\in\Sigma^{\prime}\land\varepsilon^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}

is a ghost command, we know

\mbox{{(H5)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma\land o=\mathit{ok}

(H3)
, by inversion on the fold rule, and by completeness of OX consume (Prop.

(H6-11)
,
\exists i,\sigma_{f}
:

(H6)

\displaystyle p(\vec{x}_{\text{in}})(\vec{x}_{\text{out}})~\{\bigvee_{j}(\exists\vec{x}_{j}.~P_{j})\}\in\mathsf{Preds}

(H7)

\displaystyle\llbracket\vec{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\pi})\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}\triangleq\{\vec{\hat{v}}/\vec{x}_{\text{in}}\}

(H8)

\displaystyle\textsf{consume}(\textsf{OX},P_{i},\hat{\theta},(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}))\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}))

(H9)

\displaystyle\varepsilon,\sigma_{f}\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})=\hat{\sigma}_{f}

(H10)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\triangleq\{p(\vec{\hat{v}})(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\vec{x}_{\text{out}}))\}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f}

(H11)

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{rcl}\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})&=&(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\cdot(\hat{s},\emptyset,\{p(\vec{\hat{v}})(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\vec{x}_{\text{out}}))\},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\\
&=&\hat{\sigma}_{f}\cdot\hat{\sigma}_{p}\end{array}

where
i
is the index of the definition of the predicate that has been correctly consumed. By soundness of

(Prop.
4
),
(H6-8)
,

:

\displaystyle\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{i}\uplus\hat{h}_{f}

(H13)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{i}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f}

(H14)

\displaystyle\forall\varepsilon,\sigma.\ \varepsilon,\sigma\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{i},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{i},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\implies\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),\sigma\models P_{i}

We define

\hat{\sigma}_{i}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{i},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{i},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

We have, by definition of compositionality of symbolic states that

(H15)

\displaystyle\hat{\sigma}_{i}\cdot\hat{\sigma}_{f}=\hat{\sigma}\cdot(\emptyset,\emptyset,\emptyset,\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

In addition, from
(H4)
, the fact that
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\implies\hat{\pi}
and the fact that

(from

), we also have that

(H16)

\displaystyle\sigma\models\hat{\sigma}_{i}\cdot\hat{\sigma}_{f}

And therefore from

,
(H9)
,
(H16)
and Lem.
D.3
:
\exists\sigma_{i}.

(H17)

\displaystyle\sigma=\sigma_{i}\cdot\sigma_{f}

(H18)

\displaystyle\varepsilon,\sigma_{i}\models\hat{\sigma}_{i}

Then, by
(H14)
and
(H18)
, we have that:

(H19)

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),\sigma_{i}\models P_{i}

Then, by definition of state satisfaction for a predicate, and definition of satisfaction of a disjunction, we can derive that

(H20)

\displaystyle\varepsilon,\sigma_{i}\models\hat{\sigma}_{p}=(\hat{s},\emptyset,\{p(\vec{\hat{v}})(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\vec{x}_{\text{out}}))\},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

(H9)
,

,

,

\varepsilon,\sigma\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}

Unfolding predicates Case: C =

.

Remember the Unfold rule:

\frac{p(\vec{x}_{\text{in}})(\vec{x}_{\text{out}})~\{\bigvee_{i}(\exists\vec{x}_{i}.~P_{i})\}\in\mathsf{Preds}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\llbracket\vec{\mathsf{E}}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\pi})\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consPred}(p,\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\vec{\hat{z}}\text{ fresh}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}\triangleq\{\vec{\hat{v}}/\vec{x}_{\text{in}},\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}}/\vec{x}_{\text{out}},\vec{\hat{z}}/\vec{x}_{i}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{produce}(P_{i},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}{\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{unfold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{OX}}\mathit{ok}:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{{Unfold}}

(H1)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(H2)

\hat{\sigma},\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{OX}}\hat{\Sigma}^{\prime}

(H3)

\mathit{abort}\not\in\hat{\Sigma}^{\prime}

\varepsilon,\sigma\models\hat{\sigma}\land\sigma,\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:\sigma^{\prime}

To prove
:

\exists\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime},\varepsilon^{\prime}\geq\varepsilon.~(o,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\in\Sigma^{\prime}\land\varepsilon^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}

fold
is a ghost command, we know

\mbox{{(H5)}}\hskip 9.24994pt\sigma^{\prime}=\sigma\land o=\mathit{ok}

\displaystyle(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})=\hat{\sigma}\mbox{{Let}}~

\displaystyle(s,h)=\sigma

Since execution did not abort
(H3)
, given the two rules of

, in OX mode, we know know

(H6)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in},i};\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out},i})\mid i\in I\}\uplus\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994ptp\notin\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}

(H7)

\displaystyle\exists i.\ \texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}\land\vec{\hat{v}}=\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in},i})

(H8)

\displaystyle\texttt{UNSAT}(\hat{\pi}\wedge\neg(\bigwedge_{i\in I}\vec{\hat{v}}=\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in},i}))

From
(H4)
,
(H7)
,
(H8)
, we learn that, by choosing an adapted

, we have:
\exists\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}},\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}.

(H9)

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}};\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}})\}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}

(H10)

\displaystyle\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=(\hat{\pi}\land\vec{\hat{v}}=\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}})

(H11)

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})={\sf true}

(H12)

\displaystyle\textsf{consPred}(p,\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}},(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}))

From
(H11)
and
(H4)
, we know

(H13)

\displaystyle\varepsilon,\sigma\models(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

Moreover, by definition of composition on symbolic states, and satisfaction of symbolic states, we learn:
\exists h_{f},h_{p}.

(H14)

\displaystyle(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\cdot(\emptyset,\emptyset,\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}};\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}})\},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

(H15)

\displaystyle\varepsilon,(s,h_{f})\models(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

(H16)

\displaystyle\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{p})\models(\emptyset,\emptyset,\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}};\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}})\},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

(H17)

\displaystyle h=h_{f}\uplus h_{p}\hskip 9.24994pt(\text{which implies }{h_{f}\mathbin{\sharp}h_{p}})\

\begin{array}[]{ll@{\hspace{\tabcolsep}=\hspace{\tabcolsep}}ll}\mbox{{Let}}&\hat{\theta}_{\text{init}}\hfil\hskip 6.0pt=\hskip 6.0pt&\{\vec{\hat{v}}/\vec{x}_{\text{in}},\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}}/\vec{x}_{\text{out}}\}&\\
\mbox{{Let}}&\hat{\theta}\hfil\hskip 6.0pt=\hskip 6.0pt&\hat{\theta}_{\text{init}}\uplus\{\vec{\hat{z}}/\vec{x}_{i}\}&\vec{\hat{z}}\text{ fresh}\end{array}

From
(H11)
,
(H16)
and the definition of state satisfaction, we obtain:

\begin{array}[]{lll}&\theta_{\text{init}},(s,h_{p})\models\bigvee\limits_{i}(\exists\vec{x}_{i}.~P_{i})&\implies\\
&\exists i,\theta_{\text{init}},(s,h_{p})\models\exists\vec{x}_{i}.~P_{i}&\implies\\
&\exists i,\vec{v}.\ \theta_{\text{init}}\uplus\{\vec{v}/\vec{x}_{i}\},(s,h_{p})\models P_{i}&\implies\\
\mbox{{(H18)}}&\exists i,\varepsilon^{\prime}.~\varepsilon\leq\varepsilon^{\prime}\land\varepsilon^{\prime}(\hat{\theta}),(s,h_{p})\models P_{i}&\text{ where }\varepsilon^{\prime}=\varepsilon\uplus\{\vec{v}/\vec{\hat{z}}\}\end{array}

and
\varepsilon^{\prime}

(H15)

), and the definition of symbolic state satisfaction: we have

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mbox{{(H19)}}&\textsf{produce}(P,\hat{\theta},(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}))\rightsquigarrow(\hat{s},\hat{h}\uplus\hat{h}_{p},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{p},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\\

\end{array}

From
(H15)
,
(H21)
and
\varepsilon^{\prime}\geq\varepsilon
, we get:

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mbox{{(H22)}}&\varepsilon^{\prime},(s,h_{f})\models(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\end{array}

And therefore from
(H20)
and

\begin{array}[]{ll}\mbox{{(H23)}}&\varepsilon^{\prime},(s,h)\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}\uplus\hat{h}_{p},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{p},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\\
&\\
\mbox{{Let}}&\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}\uplus\hat{h}_{p},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{p},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\end{array}

Putting it all together.

\begin{array}[]{l@{\hspace{\tabcolsep}\text{from }}l}\hat{\sigma},\mathtt{unfold}\ p(\vec{\mathsf{E}})\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{OX}}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{s},\hat{h}\uplus\hat{h}_{p},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{p},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&\mbox{{(H9)}},\mbox{{(H10)}},\mbox{{(H11)}},\mbox{{(H12)}}\text{ and }\mbox{{(H19)}}\\
\varepsilon^{\prime},\sigma^{\prime}\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}\hfil\hskip 6.0pt&\mbox{{(H5)}}\text{ and }\mbox{{(H23)}}\end{array}

∎

Appendix E
Consume and Produce Implementation: Definitions and Correctness

This appendix provides the proofs of correctness for our
consume
and
produce
implementations. We start by providing the exhaustive definitions, algorithms, and rules of our implementation and then give the proofs in question.

E.1
Rules for
consume

In this section we present the complete set of rules for
consume
: auxiliary rules in Fig.
10
, the successful rules in Fig.
11
, and the error rules in Fig.
12
.

\frac{\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{f}\uplus\{\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}\}\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\pi}\wedge(\hat{v}=\hat{v}_{1})\hskip 8.50012pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}{\textsf{consCell}(\hat{v},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow({\hat{v}_{2}},\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{heap}:=\hat{h}_{f},\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])}\frac{\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}\wedge v\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{h}))}{\textsf{consCell}(\hat{v},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow abort}

\frac{\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}};\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}})\}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\triangleq\hat{\pi}\wedge\vec{\hat{v}}=\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}}\hskip 17.00024pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}{\textsf{consPred}(p,\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}},\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{preds}:=\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])}\frac{\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in},i};\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out},i})\mid i\in I\}\uplus\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\hskip 8.50012ptp\notin\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\hskip 8.50012pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}\wedge\neg(\bigwedge_{i\in I}\vec{\hat{v}}=\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in},i}))}{\textsf{consPred}(p,\vec{\hat{v}},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow abort}

and
