\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(\textsf{OX},[(p(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins};\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs}),outs)],\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow abort([``\mathit{\sf consError}",\hat{\pi}])

Figure 12:
Rules
\mathsf{consume_{MP}}
– error rules, where
\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})

Remark E.1
.

Rules [CellPos] and [Pure] can also be applied when
outs=[]
. In such cases,
\hat{\theta}=\hat{\theta}^{\prime}
, i.e., the initial substitution already covers the assertion and does not need to be extended.

E.2
Correctness of
consume

Suppose

\begin{array}[]{r@{~\rightsquigarrow~}l}\textsf{consume}(m,P,\hat{\theta},(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}))&(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},(\hat{s}^{\prime},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}))\end{array}

Theorem E.1

(Property
1
)
.

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}))
and
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime})
hold.

Proof.

We assume the following:

(A1a)

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}))\triangleq\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}))\wedge\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\mathcal{P}})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi})
, where
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}))\triangleq(\mathtt{sv}(\hat{s})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{h})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}))\land\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi})\land\hat{\pi}\models(\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s})\land\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h}))

(A1b)

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{\theta},\hat{\pi})\triangleq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi})\wedge\hat{\pi}\models\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{\theta})

(A1c)

\mathtt{lv}(P)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{\theta})

The proof now follows by induction on
P
:

(
P
is pure)

In this case,
\textsf{plan}(P,\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{\theta}))=(P,outs)
.

The interesting case is for
outs(P)\neq[]
.

\frac{P\text{ is pure }\hskip 9.24994ptouts=[(\mathit{x}_{i},\mathit{E}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}]\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}^{\prime}=\hat{\theta}\uplus\{(\mathit{x}_{i}\mapsto\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{i}))|_{i=1}^{n}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consPure}(m,\hat{\pi},\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(P))=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}}{\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(m,[(P,outs)],\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}]))}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{left=(Pure)}

Thus,
\hat{h}_{f}=\hat{h}
and
\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f}=\hat{\mathcal{P}}
. Depending on the mode we have:

•

if
m=\textsf{UX}
then
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(P)
and
\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
.

•

if
m=\textsf{OX}
then
\hat{\pi}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}
and
\hat{\pi}\models\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(P)

Notice that

(H1)
the resulting state is well-formed. i.e.,
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}))
. It follows directly from the initial assumption
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}))
and the shape of
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}
.

(H2)

\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})=\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{i}))\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
, from (A1b).

(H3)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\models\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})
.

In fact, for each
\varepsilon
s.t.
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})={\sf true}
, it follows that
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}
, which implies, by (A1c) that
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\models\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{\theta})
holds. Since
\hat{\theta}^{\prime}=\hat{\theta}\uplus\{(x_{i}\mapsto\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{i}))|_{i=1}^{n}\}
, it follows that
\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{i})\in\mathsf{Val}
. Thus,
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})
holds.

The result follows from
(H1)
,
(H2)
and
(H3)
.

(
P
is spatial)

P=\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v}
(The case
P=\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\varnothing
is similar.)

In this case,
\textsf{plan}(P,\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{\theta}))=(P,outs)
.

The interesting case is for
outs(P)\neq[]
.

\frac{\textsf{consPure}(m,\hat{\pi},\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a})\in\mathsf{Val})=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consCell}(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a}),\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])\rightsquigarrow(\hat{v},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}_{subst}=\{\mathsf{O}\mapsto\hat{v}\}\hskip 9.24994ptouts=[(\mathit{x}_{i},\mathit{E}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}]\hskip 9.24994pt((\hat{\theta}\uplus\hat{\theta}_{subst})(E_{i})=\hat{v}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}^{\prime}=\hat{\theta}\uplus\{(x_{i}\mapsto\hat{v}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consPure}(m,(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}).\mathsf{pc},\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v})=\hat{v})=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}}{\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(m,[(\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v},outs)],\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}])}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{left=(CellPos)}

If we analyse each mode separately:

•

if
m=\textsf{UX}
then
consCell
gives
\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{f}\cdot\{\hat{v}_{a}\mapsto\hat{v}\}
,
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{a})=\hat{v}_{a}
and
\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
. Besides,
\textsf{consPure}(\textsf{UX},(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}).\mathsf{pc},\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v})=\hat{v})=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}
gives

\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\wedge\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v})=\hat{v}
and
\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
.

•

if
m=\textsf{OX}
then
consCell
gives
\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{f}\cdot\{\hat{v}_{a}\mapsto\hat{v}\}
,
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\pi}
and
\hat{\pi}\models\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{a})=\hat{v}
. Besides,
\textsf{consPure}(\textsf{OX},(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}).\mathsf{pc},\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v})=\hat{v})=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}
gives
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}
and
\hat{\pi}\models\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v})=\hat{v}
.

(H1)
the resulting state is well-formed, i.e.,
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}))
. This follows directly from the construction above.

(H2)

\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
.

In fact, by definition,
\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})=\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta})\cup\bigcup_{i}\mathtt{sv}(\hat{v}_{i})
. From the construction above
\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v})=\hat{v}
and
\mathtt{sv}(\hat{v})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{h})
. For
UX
: first,
\mathtt{sv}(\hat{v})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
; second, from (A1b),
\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi})
. For
OX
: first
\hat{\pi}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}
; second,
\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})=\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{v})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{h})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi})
.

(H3)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\models\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})
.

For
UX
: In fact, for each
\varepsilon
s.t.
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})={\sf true}
, it follows that
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}
, which implies, by (A1c) that
\hat{\pi}\models\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{\theta})
holds. Since
\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
,
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\wedge\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v})=\hat{v}
and
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h})
we get
\hat{v}\in\mathsf{Val}
. Therefore,
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})
holds. For
OX
: the analysis is similar.

The result follows from
(H1)
,
(H2)
and
(H3)
.

(Predicates)

P=p(\vec{\mathit{E}_{1}})(\vec{\mathit{E}_{2}})
this case is analogous to the previous.

(Inductive Step)

P=P_{1}\mathrel{\star}P_{2}

This case follows by the induction hypothesis.

∎

Theorem E.2

(Property
2
)
.

\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\implies\hat{\pi}
holds.

Proof.

The analysis depends on the
mode
used.

•

mode=\textsf{UX}

By inspection of the rules for
consume
it is easy to see that

\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}
,
for some
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}
such that
\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
, which trivially gives
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\implies\hat{\pi}
.

•

mode=\textsf{OX}

This case is trivial since when the path condition is modified in
OX
it is of the form
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}
, for some
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}
.

∎

Theorem E.3

(Property
3
)
.

\hat{\theta}^{\prime}\geq\hat{\theta}\,\wedge\,\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})\supseteq\mathtt{lv}(P)
.

Proof.

By hypothesis,
\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{\theta})=\{\vec{\mathit{x}}\}\subseteq\mathtt{lv}(\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P)
.
By inspecting the rules, it is easy to check that
\hat{\theta}^{\prime}
extends
\hat{\theta}
.

It remains to prove that

\mathtt{lv}(P)\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})
. Suppose that there exists
y\in\mathtt{lv}(P)
such that

y\notin dom(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})
. Then, clearly
y\notin\vec{\mathit{x}}
and there exists a
simple assertion
P_{i}
in the composition of
P
such that
y\in\mathtt{lv}(P_{i})
.
There are two cases to consider:

1.

P_{i}
is a pure assertion.

The interesting case is for equality
E_{1}=E_{2}
and
y\in\mathtt{lv}(E_{2})
. The case for
y\in\mathtt{lv}(E_{1})

can be verified analogously.
Since
\textsf{plan}(\textit{KB},P)=mp
, there exists some ordering of the simple assertions in

P
such that at least one of the
E_{i}
’s is known and

\textsf{insOuts}(E_{1}=E_{2})=(\mathtt{lv}(E_{1},E_{2})\setminus\mathtt{lv}(outs),outs)
.
Therefore,
ins=\mathtt{lv}(E_{1},E_{2})\setminus\mathtt{lv}(outs)\subseteq\textit{KB}

and
(E_{1}=E_{2},outs)
is in
mp
.

•

If
outs=[]
then
y\in ins=\mathtt{lv}(E_{1},E_{2})\subseteq\textit{KB}
,
which implies that
y
was added in
KB
in a previous point of the search.
Then, there exists a simple assertion
P_{j}
that occurs before
E_{1}=E_{2}
such that
(P_{j},[(y,\mathit{E}_{j})])
is in
mp
. In that case, since

consume
is successful,
[y\mapsto E_{j}]
was added to the substitution

\hat{\theta}
after one application of rule [Pure], i.e,
y\in\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})
. Contradiction.

•

If
outs\neq[]
and
y\notin ins=\mathtt{lv}(E_{1},E_{2})\subseteq\textit{KB}
, then

(y,\mathit{E}^{\prime})
is in
outs
for some
E^{\prime}
. With an application
of [Pure] in
consume
, which gives a contradiction as above.

2.

P_{i}
is a spatial assertion.

The interesting case is for
P_{i}
of the form
\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto E_{v}
and
y\in\mathtt{lv}(\mathit{E}_{a})
. The case for
y\in\mathtt{lv}(\mathit{E}_{v})

can be verified analogously.

In the computation of the
MP

mp
for
P
(by hypothesis it exists) we compute
(ins,outs)=\textsf{insOuts}(\textit{KB}^{\prime},\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v})

where
\textit{KB}_{i}=\textit{KB}\cup V
, and
V
is a possibly empty set of logical variables
that were added in a previous point in the computation of
mp
.

Thus, there must exist an ordering of the simple assertions of
P
such that
\mathtt{lv}(\mathit{E}_{a})\in\textit{KB}_{i}
,
in which case one has
(ins,outs)=(\mathtt{lv}(\mathit{E}_{a},\mathit{E}_{v})\setminus{\mathtt{lvar}}(outs),outs)
and

outs=\textsf{learnExp}(\textit{KB}_{i},\mathsf{O},\mathit{E}_{v})
.

•

If
outs=[]
and it is the case that
\mathtt{lv}(\mathit{E}_{v})\subseteq\textit{KB}^{\prime}
, then

y\in\mathtt{lv}(E_{2})\subseteq\textit{KB}_{i}
. This implies that there exists a simple
assertion
P_{j}
and
\textit{KB}_{j}
(for some
i>j
) such that
(P_{j},outs_{j})
is in
mp
and
(y,\mathit{E}_{j})
is in

outs_{j}
.

•

Otherwise,
[]\neq outs=[(y,\mathit{E}_{v}^{\prime})]

for some
\mathit{E}_{v}^{\prime}
obtained after manipulating
\mathit{E}_{v}
.

In both cases, since
consume
is successful,
\hat{\theta}
will be extended with
[y\mapsto\_]
, where
\_
denotes
either
E_{v}^{\prime}
or
E_{j}
. Contradiction.

∎

Lemma E.4
.

Let
P
be a pure assertion. Then,

\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon(\hat{\theta})}=\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}(P))
.

Proof.

By induction on the structure of the pure assertion
P
.
∎

Theorem E.5

(Property
4
)
.

Soundness of
consume
holds.

\exists\hat{h}_{P},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{P}.~\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{P}\uplus\hat{h}_{f}\land\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{P}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f}\land(\forall\varepsilon,\sigma.~\varepsilon,\sigma\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{p},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{p},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\implies\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),\sigma\models P).

Proof.

Suppose
\textsf{consume}(m,P,\hat{\theta},(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}))\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}))
.
The proof is by induction on the structure of
P
.

(
P
is pure)

and not an equality.

In this case
outs=[]
and the rule [Pure] from Fig.
11
is instantiated as:

\frac{P\text{ is pure }\hskip 18.49988pt\textsf{consPure}(m,\hat{\pi},\hat{\theta}(P))=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}}{\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(m,(P,[]),\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime})]}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{left=(Pure)}

Then,
\hat{h}_{f}=\hat{h}
and
\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f}=\hat{\mathcal{P}}
which gives
\hat{h}_{P}=\emptyset

and
\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{P}=\emptyset
.

The rest of the analysis relies on the mode used:

•

if
m=\textsf{UX}
then
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(P)
and
\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
.

Let
\varepsilon
and
\sigma=(s,h)
such that
\varepsilon,\sigma\models(\hat{s},\emptyset,\emptyset,\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
. Then,
\varepsilon(\hat{s})=s
,
\varepsilon(\hat{h})=h=\emptyset
and
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})=\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(P))={\sf true}
. By definition, the following holds:

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),(s,\emptyset)\models P

\displaystyle\iff\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),(s,\emptyset)\models P

\displaystyle\iff\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon(\hat{\theta})}{}={\sf true}\wedge{\sf emp}

\displaystyle\iff\varepsilon(\hat{\theta})(P)={\sf true}\wedge{\sf emp}~\text{ (Lemma~\ref{lem:esem_comm})}

which trivially follows from the hypothesis that
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(P))={\sf true}
.

•

if
m=\textsf{OX}
then
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\pi}
and
\hat{\pi}\models\hat{\theta}(P)
.

Let
\varepsilon
and
\sigma=(s,h)
be such that
\varepsilon,\sigma\models(\hat{s},\emptyset,\emptyset,\hat{\pi})
. From
\hat{\pi}\models\hat{\theta}(P)
in
OX
, we have
\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}(P))={\sf true}
and the result follows similarly to the case above.

(
P
is an equality)

P=(\mathit{E}_{1}=\mathit{E}_{2})

The interesting case is when
outs\neq[]
(otherwise, it is similar to the case above).

\frac{P\text{ is pure }\hskip 9.24994ptouts=[(\mathit{x}_{i},\mathit{E}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}]\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}^{\prime}=\hat{\theta}\uplus\{(\mathit{x}_{i}\mapsto\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{i}))|_{i=1}^{n}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consPure}(m,\hat{\pi},\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(P))=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}}{\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(m,[(P,outs)],\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}]))}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{left=(Pure)}

Then,
\hat{h}_{f}=\hat{h}
and
\hat{h}_{P}=\emptyset
.

Let
\varepsilon
be such that and
\sigma=(s,h)
be such that
\varepsilon,\sigma\models(\hat{s},\emptyset,\emptyset,\hat{\pi})
.
The rest of the analysis relies on the mode used and is similar to the previous case.

(
P
is an spatial assertion)

P=\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v}
.

Then, the following rule from Fig.
11
was applied

\frac{\textsf{consPure}(m,\hat{\pi},\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a})\in\mathsf{Val})=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consCell}(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a}),\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])\rightsquigarrow(\hat{v},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}_{subst}=\{\mathsf{O}\mapsto\hat{v}\}\hskip 9.24994ptouts=[(\mathit{x}_{i},\mathit{E}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}]\hskip 9.24994pt((\hat{\theta}\uplus\hat{\theta}_{subst})(E_{i})=\hat{v}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}^{\prime}=\hat{\theta}\uplus\{(x_{i}\mapsto\hat{v}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consPure}(m,(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}).\mathsf{pc},\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v})=\hat{v})=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}}{\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(m,[(\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v},outs)],\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}])}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{left=(CellPos)}

where

\frac{\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{f}\cdot\{\hat{v}_{a}\mapsto\hat{v}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}\wedge(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a})=\hat{v}_{a})\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})}{\textsf{consCell}(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a}),\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])\rightsquigarrow({\hat{v}},\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{heap}:=\hat{h}_{f},\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}])}

Take
\hat{h}_{P}=\{\hat{v}_{a}\mapsto\hat{v}\}
,
\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{P}=\emptyset
and
\hat{\sigma}_{P}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{P},\emptyset,\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
.

•

if
m=\textsf{UX}
then
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\wedge(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{a})=\hat{v}_{a})\wedge(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v})=\hat{v})
and
\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
.

Let
\varepsilon
and
\sigma=(s,h)
be such that
\varepsilon,(s,h)\models\hat{\sigma}_{P}
.
Then,
\varepsilon(\hat{s})=s
,
\varepsilon(\hat{h}_{P})=h
, and
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})={\sf true}
, which implies

(H1)

\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{a})=\hat{v}_{a})={\sf true}
and

\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v})=\hat{v})={\sf true}
.
By definition,

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),(s,h)\models\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v}

\displaystyle\iff\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),(s,h)\models\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v}

\displaystyle\iff\varepsilon(\hat{h}_{p})=\{\llbracket{\mathit{E}_{a}}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})}\mapsto\llbracket{\mathit{E}_{v}}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})}\},\text{ by {(H1)}}

and the result follows.

•

if
mode=\textsf{OX}
then the proof is analogous.

(
P
is a predicate assertion)

P=p(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{in}};\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{out}})

Then, the following rule from Fig.
11
was applied

\frac{\textsf{consPure}(m,\hat{\pi},\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{in}})\subseteq\mathsf{Val})=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consPred}(p,\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{in}}),\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])\rightsquigarrow(\vec{\hat{v}}_{outs},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994ptouts=[(x_{i},\mathit{E}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}]\hskip 9.24994pt\vec{\hat{v}}_{outs}=[\hat{v}_{o_{1}},\dots,\hat{v}_{o_{k}}]\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}_{subst}=\{({\sf O}_{i}\mapsto\hat{v}_{o_{i}})|_{i=1}^{k}\}\hskip 9.24994pt((\hat{\theta}\uplus\hat{\theta}_{subst})(E_{i})=\hat{v}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}^{\prime}=\hat{\theta}\uplus\{(x_{i}\mapsto\hat{v}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consPure}(m,(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}).\mathsf{pc},\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{out}})=\vec{\hat{v}}_{outs})=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}}{\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(m,[(p(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{in}};\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{out}}),outs)],\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}])}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{left=(ConsPred)}

where

\frac{\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}};\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}})\}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\triangleq\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{in}})=\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}}\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})}{\textsf{consPred}(p,\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{in}}),\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])\rightsquigarrow(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}},\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{preds}:=\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f},\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}])}

Then
\hat{h}_{f}=\hat{h}
, since this case does not affect
the symbolic heap, which gives
\hat{h}_{P}=\emptyset
. Also,
\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{P}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f}
where

\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{P}=\{p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}};\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}})\}
.

The rest of the proof depends on the mode.

•

m=\textsf{UX}

By the construction
above
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{in}})=\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}}\wedge\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{out}})=\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}}
.

Let
\varepsilon,\sigma=(s,h)
be such that

\varepsilon,\sigma\models(\emptyset,\emptyset,\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{P},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})
. Then, by
definition

\displaystyle\varepsilon,\sigma\models(\emptyset,\emptyset,\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{P},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})

\displaystyle\iff s=\emptyset\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})={\sf true}\wedge\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h)\models\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{P}

\displaystyle\iff s=\emptyset\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})={\sf true}\wedge\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h)\models p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}};\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}})

Now, from the assumption that
p(\vec{x}_{\text{in}};\vec{x}_{\text{out}})~\{P^{\prime}\}\in\mathsf{Preds}

and the definition of
\models
:

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),\sigma\models p(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{in}};\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{out}})

\displaystyle\iff\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})[\vec{x}_{\text{in}}\mapsto\llbracket{\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{in}}}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),\emptyset},\vec{x}_{\text{out}}\mapsto\llbracket{\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{out}}}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),\emptyset}],(\emptyset,h)\models P^{\prime}

\displaystyle\iff\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})[\vec{x}_{\text{in}}\mapsto\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{in}}),\vec{x}_{\text{out}}\mapsto\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{out}})],(\emptyset,h)\models P^{\prime}

\displaystyle\iff\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})[\vec{x}_{\text{in}}\mapsto\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}},\vec{x}_{\text{out}}\mapsto\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}}],(\emptyset,h)\models P^{\prime}

and the result follows from
\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h)\models p(\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{in}};\vec{\hat{v}}_{\text{out}})

above.

•

m=\textsf{OX}

This case is similar.

(
P
is a star-conjunction)

P=P_{1}\mathrel{\star}P_{2}\mathrel{\star}\ldots\mathrel{\star}P_{n}
(for some
n
).

This case follows easily by induction hypothesis.

Theorem E.6

(Property
5
)
.

Completeness of
consume
in
OX
mode:

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathit{abort}\not\in\textsf{consume}(\textsf{OX},P,\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\land\varepsilon,\sigma\models\hat{\sigma}\implies\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\exists\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\sigma_{f},\hat{\sigma}_{f}.~\textsf{consume}(\textsf{OX},P,\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}_{f})\land\varepsilon,\sigma_{f}\models\hat{\sigma}_{f}\end{array}

Proof.

Let
\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})
be a symbolic state and
\varepsilon
and

\sigma=(s,h)
be such that
\varepsilon,\sigma\models\hat{\sigma}
.
Since
abort
is not an outcome for
consume
, none of the rules in Fig.
12
were applied and
\textsf{plan}(\hat{\theta},P)

does not fail. The proof is by induction on the structure of
P
.

(
P
is pure)

Then
\textsf{plan}(\hat{\theta},P)=(P,outs)
.

The interesting case is for
outs\neq[]
. The rule applied is:

\frac{P\text{ is pure }\hskip 9.24994ptouts=[(\mathit{x}_{i},\mathit{E}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}]\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}^{\prime}=\hat{\theta}\uplus\{(\mathit{x}_{i}\mapsto\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{i}))|_{i=1}^{n}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consPure}(\textsf{OX},\hat{\pi},\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(P))=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}}{\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(\textsf{OX},[(P,outs)],\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{left=(Pure)}

Then,
\hat{\sigma}_{f}=\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}]=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
.
Since
m=\textsf{OX}
then
\hat{\pi}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}
and the result follows trivially.

(
P
is spatial)

P=\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v}

The interesting case happens for
\mathit{E}_{v}\neq\varnothing.

Then, the rule [CellPos] from Fig.
11
was applied

\frac{\textsf{consPure}(\textsf{OX},\hat{\pi},\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a})\in\mathsf{Nat})=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consCell}(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a}),\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])\rightsquigarrow(\hat{v},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}_{subst}=\{\mathsf{O}\mapsto\hat{v}\}\hskip 9.24994ptouts=[(\mathit{x}_{i},\mathit{E}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}]\hskip 9.24994pt((\hat{\theta}\uplus\hat{\theta}_{subst})(E_{i})=\hat{v}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\theta}^{\prime}=\hat{\theta}\uplus\{(x_{i}\mapsto\hat{v}_{i})|_{i=1}^{n}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{consPure}(\textsf{OX},(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}).\mathsf{pc},\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v})=\hat{v})=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}}{\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(\textsf{OX},[(\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v},outs)],\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}])}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{left=(CellPos)}

where

\frac{\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{f}\uplus\{\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}\}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\wedge(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a})=\hat{v}_{1})\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})}{\textsf{consCell}(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a}),\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])\rightsquigarrow({\hat{v}},\underbrace{\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{heap}:=\hat{h}_{f},\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}]}_{\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}})}

Note that
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\pi}
and
\neg(\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}\wedge\neg(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{a})\in\mathsf{Nat}))

by definition of
consPure
.
From
consCell
, we have
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a})=\hat{v}_{1}
. From the last
consPure
call,
\neg(\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\wedge\neg(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v})=\hat{v}))
and
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}
.
By soundness of
consume
, we have
(H1)

\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{f}\uplus\hat{h}_{P}
,
where
\hat{h}_{P}=\{\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}\}
.

By definition of
\models
:

\displaystyle\mbox{{(H2)}}~\varepsilon,(s,h)\models\hat{\sigma}

\displaystyle\iff\exists h_{1},h_{2}.~h=h_{1}\uplus h_{2}\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{s})=s\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{h})=h_{1}\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}

\displaystyle\wedge\varepsilon,(s,h_{2})\models\hat{\mathcal{P}}

Take
\hat{\sigma}_{f}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
.
We will show that
\varepsilon,(s,h_{f}\uplus h_{2})\models\hat{\sigma}_{f}
. In fact, from
(H1)
and
(H2)
, it follows that there exist
h_{P},h_{f}
such that

h_{1}=h_{f}\uplus h_{P}
and
(H3)

\varepsilon(\hat{h}_{f})=h_{f}\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{h}_{P})=h_{P}
. Notice that there exists at least one branch (in
consCell
) such that
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})=\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{a})=\hat{v}_{1})={\sf true}
, otherwise we would have
\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{a})\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{h})
and
consCell
, therefore
consume
, would
abort
, and this contradicts our initial hypothesis. Therefore,

\displaystyle\exists h_{f},h_{2}.~{h_{2}\mathbin{\sharp}h_{f}}\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{h}_{f})=h_{f}\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})={\sf true}\wedge\varepsilon,(s,h_{2})\models\hat{\mathcal{P}}

and the result follows for

\sigma_{f}=(s,h_{f}\uplus h_{2})
, i.e.,
\varepsilon,\sigma_{f}\models\hat{\sigma}_{f}
.

(
P
is a predicate assertion)

P=p(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{in}};\vec{\mathit{E}}_{\text{out}})

This case follows similarly to the previous case.

∎

Theorem E.7

(Property
6
)
.

Completeness of UX
consume
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\textsf{consume}(\textsf{UX},A,\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\,\wedge\,\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),(\emptyset,h_{A})\models A\wedge{h^{\prime}\mathbin{\sharp}h_{A}}\,\wedge\,\varepsilon,(s,h^{\prime})\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\implies\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{A})\models\hat{\sigma}_{A}\,\wedge\,\varepsilon,(s,h^{\prime}\uplus h_{A})\models\hat{\sigma}\end{array}

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
A
.

Let
\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})
. We have the following:

1.

\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{A}\uplus\hat{h}_{f}
and
\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{A}\uplus\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{f}
(soundness of UX
consume
)

2.

\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}.\mathsf{pc}=\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\pi}^{\prime}
, for some
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}
(definition of UX
consume
)

The remaining of the proof is done after the planning phase, considering the
consume
of the
MP
obtained from
A
, which we will denote as
\textit{MP}(A)
.

Thus, we analyse the rules for
\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(\textsf{UX},[\textit{MP}(A)],\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})
.

(
A
is pure)

(
A
is an spatial assertion)

A=\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v}
.

}\textsf{consPure}(\textsf{UX},(\hat{\sigma}_{f}).\mathsf{pc},\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v})=\hat{v})=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}\text{consume cell contents}\lx@close@alignment\end{equation*}and,inthiscase,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\hat{\sigma}_{f}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}].\par Thedefinitionof\textsf{consPure}implies\begin{enumerate}\enumerate@item@$\exists\hat{v}_{a},\hat{v},\hat{h}_{f}.~\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{f}\uplus\{\hat{v}_{a}\mapsto\hat{v}\}$

\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@$\varepsilon,(s,h^{\prime})\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}$
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@By definition of $\models$:
\par$$\begin{aligned} \varepsilon,(s,h^{\prime})\models\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\Leftrightarrow\exists h_{1}^{\prime},h_{2}^{\prime}.~&h^{\prime}=h^{\prime}_{1}\uplus h^{\prime}_{2}\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{h}_{f})=h_{1}^{\prime}\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{s})=s\\
&\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})={\sf true}\wedge\varepsilon,(s,h_{2}^{\prime})\models\hat{\mathcal{P}}\end{aligned}$$\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\enumerate@item@By definition of $\models$:
\par$$\begin{aligned} \varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),(\emptyset,h_{A})\models\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v}&\Leftrightarrow h_{A}=\{\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{a}))\mapsto\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(\mathit{E}_{v}))\}\\
&\Leftrightarrow h_{A}=\{\varepsilon(\hat{v}_{a})\mapsto\varepsilon(\hat{v}_{v})\}\end{aligned}$$\end{enumerate}\par From\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})={\sf true},itfollowsthat\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{A})\models\hat{\sigma}_{A},bydefinition.\par\par From(8){h^{\prime}\mathbin{\sharp}h_{A}},incombinationwith(4),(10)and(11)wehave$$\begin{aligned} \varepsilon,(s,h^{\prime}\uplus h_{A})\models\hat{\sigma}&\iff\varepsilon,(s,h^{\prime}\uplus h_{A})\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{f}\uplus\hat{h}_{A},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})\\
&\iff\exists h_{1}^{\prime},h_{2}^{\prime}.~h^{\prime}=h^{\prime}_{1}\uplus h^{\prime}_{2}\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{h}_{f})=h_{1}^{\prime}\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{h}_{A})=h_{A}\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{s})=s\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt\wedge\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}\wedge\varepsilon,(s,h_{2}^{\prime})\models\hat{\mathcal{P}}\end{aligned}$$andtheresultfollows.\end{description}\qed\end@proof\par\par\begin{example}Suppose the symbolic execution is in a state $\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{H},\hat{\pi})$ with the initial substitution is $\hat{\theta}=\{50/c,1/x\}$ and $P=x\mapsto v$ that we want to consume. Let $\hat{H}=(\{\hat{\mathit{x}}\mapsto\hat{\mathit{c}},\hat{\mathit{y}}\mapsto 1,3\mapsto 5\},\emptyset)$ be the symbolic resource, and $\hat{\pi}=\hat{\mathit{c}}\geq 42\land\hat{\mathit{x}}\neq\hat{\mathit{y}}\land\hat{\mathit{x}},\hat{\mathit{y}}\in\mathsf{Nat}$ be symbolic path condition (the symbolic store $\hat{s}$ is irrelevant to this computation and left opaque).
We will use our consume implementation:
\par We use the ${\sf CellPos}$, in Fig.~\ref{fig:mac-success}, that requires to check:
\begin{itemize}\itemize@item@$\textsf{consPure}(\textsf{UX},\hat{\pi},\hat{\theta}(x)\in\mathsf{Val})=\hat{\pi}\land 1\in\mathsf{Val}$.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@conscell branches:
\begin{description}\description@item@[(branch 1)]$\textsf{consCell}(1,\hat{\sigma}[pc:=\hat{\pi}\land 1\in\mathsf{Val}])\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\mathit{c}},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})$, where $\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\hat{\sigma}[heap:=\hat{h}_{f},pc:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}]$, with $\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{f}\uplus\{\hat{\mathit{x}}\mapsto\hat{\mathit{c}}\}$, $\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land(\hat{\mathit{x}}=1)$ and $\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})$.
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\description@item@[(branch 2)]$\textsf{consCell}(1,\hat{\sigma}[pc:=\hat{\pi}\land 1\in\mathsf{Val}])\rightsquigarrow(1,\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},pc:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}])$, where $\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=\hat{\sigma}[heap:=\hat{h}_{f}^{\prime},pc:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}]$ with $\hat{h}=\hat{h}_{f}^{\prime}\uplus\{\hat{\mathit{y}}\mapsto 1\}$, $\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land(\hat{\mathit{y}}=1)$ and $\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})$.
\end{description}\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\hat{\theta}_{subst}$ for each branch:
\begin{description}\description@item@[(branch 1)]$\hat{\theta}_{subst}=\{\hat{\mathit{c}}/{\sf O}\}$ and {\bf(branch 2)}~ $\hat{\theta}_{subst}=\{1/{\sf O}\}$
\end{description}\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$outs=[(v,{\sf O}]$
\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\hat{\theta}^{\prime}$ for each branch:
\begin{description}\description@item@[(branch 1)]$\hat{\theta}^{\prime}=\hat{\theta}\uplus\{\hat{\mathit{c}}/v\}$ and {\bf(branch 2)} $\hat{\theta}^{\prime}=\hat{\theta}\uplus\{1/v\}$
\par\end{description}\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\itemize@item@$\textsf{consPure}(\textsf{UX},(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}).pc,\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(v)=\hat{\mathit{v}})$ for each branch:
\begin{description}\description@item@[(branch 1)]$\textsf{consPure}(\textsf{UX},(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}).pc,\hat{\mathit{c}}=\hat{\mathit{c}})=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land(\hat{\mathit{x}}=1)$
\par\par\vskip 4.0pt\vskip 4.0pt plus 2.0pt minus 1.0pt\description@item@[(branch 2)]$\textsf{consPure}(\textsf{UX},(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}).pc,1=1)=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land(\hat{\mathit{y}}=1)$
\end{description}\end{itemize}Thus,
$$\begin{aligned} \mathsf{consume_{MP}}(\textsf{UX},[(x\mapsto v,outs)],\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})&\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land\hat{\mathit{x}}=1])\hskip 18.49988pt{\bf(branch1)}\\
&\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land\hat{\mathit{y}}=1])\hskip 18.49988pt{\bf(branch2)}\\
\end{aligned}$$The computation of produce is in Ex.~\ref{app:ex-produce}.
\par\end{example}\par\begin{example}Consider the consume step in Example~\ref{ex:fcall-ux}:
$$\textsf{consume}(\textsf{UX},x\mapsto v,\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow(\hat{\theta}\cup\{\hat{\mathit{c}}/v\},(\hat{s},(\{\hat{\mathit{y}}\mapsto 1,3\mapsto 5\},\emptyset),\hat{\pi}\land\hat{\mathit{x}}=1))$$The substitution is
$\hat{\theta}^{\prime}=\hat{\theta}\cup\{\hat{\mathit{c}}/v\}$, the assertion is $P=x\mapsto v$ and the consumed resource is $\hat{\sigma}_{P}=(\emptyset,(\{\hat{\mathit{x}}\mapsto\hat{\mathit{c}}\},\emptyset),\hat{\pi}\land\hat{\mathit{x}}=1)$.
For Property~\ref{prop:soundness}, consider the decomposition of resource $\hat{H}$ into the parts $\hat{H}_{P}=(\{\hat{\mathit{x}}\mapsto\hat{\mathit{c}}\},\emptyset)$ and $\hat{H}_{f}=(\{\hat{\mathit{y}}\mapsto 1,3\mapsto 5\},\emptyset)$. Take the interpretation $\varepsilon=\{1/\hat{\mathit{x}},3/\hat{\mathit{y}},42/\hat{\mathit{c}}\}$.
By definition of interpretation of symbolic states (\lx@sectionsign\ref{sub:funspec}), $\varepsilon,(\emptyset,\{1\mapsto 42\})\models\hat{\sigma}_{P}$ holds if and only if $\varepsilon((\emptyset,\{\hat{\mathit{x}}\mapsto\hat{\mathit{c}}\}))=(\emptyset,\{1\mapsto 42\})$ and $\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\mathit{x}}=1)={\sf true}$, and to see that the latter holds, just apply the $\varepsilon$ to the symbolic variables and verify the equality. Thus, $\varepsilon,(\emptyset,\{1\mapsto 42\})$ is a model of $\hat{\sigma}_{P}$. Also, note that $\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})=\{1/x,5/c,42/v\}$ and $\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),(\emptyset,\{1\mapsto 42\})\models P$ holds. Therefore, $\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),(\emptyset,\{1\mapsto 42\})$ is a model of $P$.
\par\par For Property~\ref{prop:mac-ux-comp}, consider an interpretation $\varepsilon$ such that $\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\mathit{x}}=1)={\sf true}$ and $\varepsilon(\hat{\mathit{c}})=50$. In addition, consider the concrete state $(\emptyset,\{1\mapsto 50\})$. Then $\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime})=\{1/x,50/c,50/v\}$, and by definition of satisfaction (App.~\ref{appssec:furtherdef}), the judgement $\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}^{\prime}),(\emptyset,\{1\mapsto 50\})\models x\mapsto v$ holds. Thus, the premises of Property~\ref{prop:mac-ux-comp} are satisfied. The following holds $\varepsilon,(\emptyset,\{1\mapsto 50\})\models(\emptyset,(\{\hat{\mathit{x}}\mapsto\hat{\mathit{c}}\},\emptyset),\hat{\pi}\land\hat{\mathit{x}}=1)$ iff $\varepsilon((\emptyset,\{\hat{\mathit{x}}\mapsto\hat{\mathit{c}}\},\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\mathit{x}}=1))=(\emptyset,\{1\mapsto 50\})$ as $\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}\land\hat{\mathit{x}}=1)={\sf true}$.
\end{example}\par

Example E.10

(Produce computation of Ex.
2
)
.

In this example, we give the complete computation of the produce step used in Ex.
2
. Consider the symbolic state
\hat{\sigma}_{f}=(\hat{s},(\{\hat{\mathit{y}}\mapsto 1,3\mapsto 5\},\emptyset),\hat{\pi}\land\hat{\mathit{x}}=1)
, with the notations
\hat{H}_{f}
for the symbolic heap and
\hat{\pi}_{f}
for the symbolic path condition. The substitution is
\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\theta}^{\prime}\cup\{1/x,50/c,\hat{\mathit{c}}/v,\hat{\mathit{r}}/r\}
, with
r,\hat{\mathit{r}}
are fresh variables. The post-condition to be produced is
Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime\prime}=x\mapsto c\mathrel{\star}c\geq 42\mathrel{\star}r=v
. First, we apply the rule to produce cell assertions, which adds to the path condition
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\pi}_{f}\land\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(x)\in\mathsf{Nat}\land\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(c)\in\mathsf{Nat}
and check that it is
SAT
. Second, we apply
prodCell
rule which checks that
1\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{H}_{f})
, updates the path condition to
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land 1\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{H}_{f})\land 50\in\mathsf{Val}
and extends the symbolic heap with
\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(x)\mapsto\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(c)
obtaining a new symbolic state
(\hat{s},\hat{H}_{f}\cup(\{1\mapsto 50\},\emptyset),\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
. Finally, we have to produce the pure assertions
c\geq 42\mathrel{\star}r=v
and these only add to the symbolic path condition
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\land\hat{\theta}(c)\geq 42\land\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(r)=\hat{\theta}^{\prime}(v)
which is the same as
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\land 50\geq 42\land\hat{\mathit{r}}=\hat{\mathit{c}}

The produce step for
(branch 2)
is computed analogously.

E.4
Correctness of
produce

Suppose

{\sf produce}(Q,\hat{\theta},(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}))\rightsquigarrow(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

and assume

(A1)

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}),\hat{\theta})
;

(A3)

\hat{\theta}
covers
P
.

Theorem E.11

(Property
1
for
{\tt produce}
)
.

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime}))

Proof.

By induction on the structure of
Q
and also assuming

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}),\hat{\theta})
which we recall below:

\displaystyle\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}),\hat{\theta})

\displaystyle\triangleq\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}))\wedge\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi})\,\wedge\,\hat{\pi}\models\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{\theta})

\displaystyle\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}))

\displaystyle\triangleq(\mathtt{sv}(\hat{s})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\mathcal{P}})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{h})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}))\wedge\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi})\wedge\hat{\pi}\models(\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s})\wedge\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h}))

There are some cases to consider:

(
Q
is pure)

In this case, the following rule from Fig.
13
was applied

P\text{ is pure}\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=(\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}P)\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

\textsf{produce}(P,\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}]

Notice that

(H1)

\mathtt{sv}(\hat{s})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{h})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\mathcal{P}})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
.

A consequence of the
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}))
hypothesis and the fact that
\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
.

(H2)

\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
.

Trivially.

(H3)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\models(\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s})\wedge\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h}))

Now, let
\varepsilon
be such that
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})={\sf true}
. Then,
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}
and

\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})=\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}\,\wedge\,Q\hat{\theta})={\sf true}
. This trivially gives
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}
,

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})
implies
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s})\wedge\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h})
.

From
(H1)
,
(H2)
and
(H3)
one has
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
, and the result follows.

(
Q
is spatial assertion)

Q=\mathit{E}_{1}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{2}
(The case
Q=E_{1}\mapsto\varnothing
is analogous
)

In this case, the following rule from Fig.
13
was applied

\frac{\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\triangleq\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a})\in\mathsf{Nat}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{v})\in\mathsf{Val}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{prodCell}(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a}),\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{v}),\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])=\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}{\textsf{produce}(\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}

Let
\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})
. The definition of
prodCell
gives:

(H0)

\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})

(H1)

\hat{h}^{\prime}=\{\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1})\mapsto\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{2}))\}\uplus\hat{h}
;

(H2)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\wedge(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1})\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{\theta}))
; and

(H3)

\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
.

Notice that

(H4)

\mathtt{sv}(\hat{s})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{h}^{\prime})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\mathcal{P}})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
.

From
(H1)

\mathtt{sv}(\hat{h}^{\prime})=\mathtt{sv}(\hat{h})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\{\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1})\mapsto\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{2})\})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
.
The
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{\sigma},\hat{\theta})
hypothesis implies that
\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{h})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\mathcal{P}})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi})
. In combination with the fact that
\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
, the result follows.

(H5)

\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})

Trivially, from the definition of
(H3)
.

(H6)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\models(\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s})\wedge\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h}^{\prime}))

Now, let
\varepsilon
be such that
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})={\sf true}
i.e., such that

\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1})\in\mathsf{Nat}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{2})\in\mathsf{Val}\wedge(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1})\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{\theta})))={\sf true}.

Then,
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}
and
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{\sigma})
gives
\hat{\pi}\models(\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s})\wedge\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h}))
, which entails
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s})\wedge\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h})
. However, to prove
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h}^{\prime})=\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h}\uplus\{\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1})\mapsto\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{2})\})
we need to verify

\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{h}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathsf{Nat}\wedge\mathop{\mathrm{codom}}(\hat{h}^{\prime})\subseteq\mathsf{Val}\wedge(\forall v_{i},v_{j}\in\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{h}^{\prime}).i\neq j\implies v_{i}\neq v_{j})

which clearly follows from
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})={\sf true}
.

From
(H4)
,
(H5)
and
(H6)
one has
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})
, and the result follows.

(Q is a predicate assertion)

Q=p(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins};\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs})

\frac{\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\triangleq p(\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins});\theta(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs}))\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\triangleq\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins})\subseteq\mathsf{Val}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs})\subseteq\mathsf{Val}\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}{\textsf{produce}(p(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins};\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs}),\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pred}:=\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}]}

Let
\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})
and
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
.

Notice that

(H1)

\mathtt{sv}(\hat{s})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{h})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\mathcal{P}})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

From the rule,
\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime})=\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\mathcal{P}})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins}))\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs}))\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
. In addition, the
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{\sigma},\hat{\theta})
hypothesis implies that
\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\theta})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{h})\cup\mathtt{sv}(\hat{s})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(spc)
. Results hence follows from
\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi})\subseteq\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
.

(H2)

\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

Follows trivially from the rule

(H3)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\models(\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s})\land\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h}))

Follows trivially from the fact that
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}
is stronger than
\hat{\pi}
and
\hat{\pi}\models(\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{s})\land\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0mufc}(\hat{h}))
from the hypothesis
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{\sigma})
.

From
(H1-3)
, we immediately have the result.

(Inductive Step)

Q=Q_{1}\mathrel{\star}\ldots\mathrel{\star}Q_{n}
, where each
Q_{i}
is a simple assertion.

To simplify the reasoning, consider the case
n=2
. The general case is similar.

In this case, the following rule from Fig.
13
was applied

\frac{\textsf{produce}(Q_{1},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{produce}(Q_{2},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}{\textsf{produce}(Q_{1}\mathrel{\star}Q_{2},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}

From
\textsf{produce}(Q_{1},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}
and
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{\sigma},\hat{\theta})
we obtain from inductive hypothesis
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})
. Then, by applying the inductive hypothesis again, we also get that
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},ssubst)

∎

Theorem E.12

(Property
4
)
.

Soundness for
produce
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\textsf{produce}(Q,\hat{\theta},(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}))\rightsquigarrow(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\implies\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\exists\hat{h}_{q},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{q}.~\hat{h}^{\prime}=\hat{h}_{q}\uplus\hat{h}\wedge\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}=\hat{\mathcal{P}}\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{q}\land(\forall\varepsilon,\sigma.~\varepsilon,\sigma\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{q},\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{q},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\implies\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),\sigma\models Q)\end{array}

Proof.

First, we recall the assumption of well-formedness
\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}((\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}),\hat{\theta})
and the fact that Prop.
1
implies

\mathit{\mathcal{W}\mkern-2.0muf}(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
, and consequently
\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
.

The proof follows by induction on the structure of
Q
.

(
Q
is pure)

In this case,

{\sf produce}(Q,\hat{\theta},(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}))\rightsquigarrow(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

where
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(Q)
and
\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
. Also,
{\tt produce}
does not change the symbolic heap or predicates, i.e.,
\hat{h}=\hat{h}^{\prime}\land\hat{\mathcal{P}}=\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}
and we can take

\hat{h}_{q}=\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{q}=\emptyset.

Let
\varepsilon,\sigma
such that
\varepsilon,\sigma\models(\hat{s},\emptyset,\emptyset,\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
. then
\sigma=(\varepsilon(\hat{s}),\emptyset)
and
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})={\sf true}
. Therefore
\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}(Q))={\sf true}
, by definition of
\hat{\pi}
, meaning
\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),(\varepsilon(\hat{s}),\emptyset)\models Q
, proving what the property.

(
Q
is spatial)

Q=\mathit{E}_{1}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{2}
(The case
Q=\mathit{E}\mapsto\varnothing
is analogous)

In this case, the following rule from Fig.
13
was applied

\frac{\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\triangleq\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a})\in\mathsf{Nat}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{v})\in\mathsf{Val}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{prodCell}(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a}),\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{v}),\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])=\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}{\textsf{produce}(\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}

Let
(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})=\hat{\sigma}
and
(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})=\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}

The definition of
prodCell

gives
(H1)

\hat{h}^{\prime}=\{\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1})\mapsto\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{2}))\}\uplus\hat{h}
;
(H2)

\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\wedge(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1})\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{\theta}))
; and
(H3)

\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
. Take
\hat{h}_{q}=\{\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1})\mapsto\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{2}))\}
.

Let
\varepsilon
such that
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})={\sf true}
. Then, using well-formedness,
\varepsilon,(\varepsilon(\hat{s}),\varepsilon(\hat{h}_{q}))\models(\hat{s},\hat{h}_{q},\emptyset,\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
is a well-defined concrete state. By definition,

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),(\varepsilon(\hat{s}),\varepsilon(\hat{h}_{q}))\models E_{1}\mapsto E_{2}\iff\varepsilon(\hat{h}_{q})=\{\llbracket{\mathit{E}_{1}}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon(\hat{\theta})}\mapsto\llbracket{\mathit{E}_{2}}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon(\hat{\theta})}\}

which trivially holds.

(Q is a predicate assertion)

Q=p(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins};\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs})
.

\frac{\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\triangleq p(\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins});\theta(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs}))\cup\hat{\mathcal{P}}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\triangleq\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins})\subseteq\mathsf{Val}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs})\subseteq\mathsf{Val}\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})}{\textsf{produce}(p(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins};\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs}),\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pred}:=\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}]}

Let
(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})=\hat{\sigma}
and
(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})=\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}
.

We naturally pick
\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{q}=\{p(\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins});\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs}))\}
and
\hat{h}_{q}=\emptyset
.

Let
\varepsilon,\sigma
such that
\varepsilon,\sigma\models(\hat{s},\emptyset,\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{q},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})
, then by definition of satisfaction, we have that
\sigma=(s,h)
where
s=\varepsilon(\hat{s})
and
\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h)\models\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{q}
. Therefore
\varepsilon,\sigma\models p(\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins});\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs}))
, giving
\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),\sigma\models p(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins};\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs})
, proving the required property.

(Inductive Step)

Q=Q_{1}\mathrel{\star}Q_{2}

This case follows by induction hypothesis.

∎

Theorem E.13

(Property
7
)
.

Completeness of
produce
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\varepsilon,(s,h)\models\hat{\sigma}\land\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),(\emptyset,h_{p})\models P\land{h\mathbin{\sharp}h_{p}}\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\implies\exists\hat{\sigma}_{p}.\textsf{produce}(P,\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}\cdot\hat{\sigma}_{p}\land\varepsilon,(\emptyset,h_{p})\models\hat{\sigma}_{p})\end{array}

Proof.

The proof is by induction on the structure of
P
.

(
P
is pure)

Let
\hat{\sigma}
,
\varepsilon
and
\hat{\theta}
be such that

1.

\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})
;

2.

\varepsilon,(s,h)\models\hat{\sigma}
(and hence,
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi})={\sf true}
);

3.

{h\mathbin{\sharp}h_{p}}
;

4.

\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),(\emptyset,h_{p})\models P
.

By definition of satisfaction,

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),(\emptyset,h_{p})\models P

\displaystyle\iff\llbracket{P}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon(\hat{\theta})}{}={\sf true}\wedge{\sf emp}

\displaystyle\iff\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}(P))={\sf true}\wedge h_{p}=\emptyset.

Thus,
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(P))={\sf true}
and we can apply the rule for produce from Fig.
13
:

\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=(\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(P))\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})

{\sf produce}(P,\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}]

Notice that
(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})\cdot(\emptyset,\emptyset,\emptyset,\hat{\theta}(P))
and the result follows trivially.

(
P
is a spatial assertion)

P=\mathit{E}_{1}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{2}

Let
\hat{\sigma}
,
\varepsilon
and
\hat{\theta}
be such that

1.

\hat{\sigma}=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\mathcal{P}},\hat{\pi})
;

2.

{h\mathbin{\sharp}h_{p}}
;

3.

\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),(\emptyset,h_{p})\models\mathit{E}_{1}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{2}
.

By definition of satisfaction,

\displaystyle\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),(\emptyset,h_{p})\models\mathit{E}_{1}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{2}

\displaystyle\iff h_{p}=\{\llbracket{\mathit{E}_{1}}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon(\hat{\theta})}{}\mapsto\llbracket{\mathit{E}_{2}}\rrbracket_{\varepsilon(\hat{\theta})}{}\}

By definition of heaps, it follows that
\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1}))\in\mathsf{Nat}
and
\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{2}))\in\mathsf{Val}
.

Thus,
\varepsilon(\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1})\in\mathsf{Nat}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{2}))\in\mathsf{Val})={\sf true}
and we can apply the rule for produce fromFig.
13
:

\frac{\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\triangleq\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a})\in\mathsf{Nat}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{v})\in\mathsf{Val}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\textsf{prodCell}(\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{a}),\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{v}),\hat{\sigma}[\mathsf{pc}:=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}])=\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}{\textsf{produce}(\mathit{E}_{a}\mapsto\mathit{E}_{v},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}}

prodcell goes through successfuly by the hypothesis that
{h\mathbin{\sharp}h_{p}}
. Take
\hat{h}_{p}=\{\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1})\mapsto\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{2})\}
,
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
.

Notice that
(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})=(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi})\cdot(\emptyset,\hat{h}_{p},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
, where
\hat{\pi}^{\prime}=\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1})\in\mathsf{Nat}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{2})\in\mathsf{Val}\wedge\hat{\theta}(\mathit{E}_{1})\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{h})
. Also,

\varepsilon,(s,h_{p})\models((\emptyset,\hat{h}_{p},\emptyset,\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}))
and the result follows.

(P is a predicate assertion)

P=p(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins};\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs})

This case is the most trivial and does not even require the disjointness of heaps in the pre-condition.

\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}\land\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins})\subseteq\mathsf{Val}\land\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins})\subseteq\mathsf{Val})
is obtained from the
\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),(\emptyset,h_{p})\models P
, and
\hat{\sigma}_{p}
is trivially constructed as
\{p(\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{ins});\hat{\theta}(\vec{\mathit{E}}_{outs}))\}
which satisfies the property by definition of the satisfaction.

(Inductive Step)

P=P_{1}\mathrel{\star}P_{2}
and
P=P_{1}\vee P_{2}

Follow immediately from the induction hypothesis.

∎

Appendix F
Bi-abduction: Semantics and Correctness

This appendix provides a soundness proof of our bi-abduction work.

F.1
Semantics

For readability, we work with modified rules rather than the catch-fix-continue approach introduced in the main text. That is, bi-abduction can also be introduced by modifying the existing missing-resource rules of the engine (including the rules for UX
consume
) – this requires more invasive surgery but is easier to follow for the proofs.

To illustrate, e.g., consider the rule
Lookup-Err-Missing
from the symbolic semantics of the core engine. The bi-abductive version of the rule now instead of faulting extends the heap with the missing cell with a fresh symbolic variable as its value and records this cell in the anti-heap:

\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{1},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\triangleq\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land\hat{v}_{1}\in\mathsf{Nat}\land\hat{v}_{1}\not\in{\sf dom}(\hat{h})\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{v}_{2}\text{ fresh}}{(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{E}]\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\text{bi}}\mathit{ok}:((\hat{s}[\mathsf{x}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\land\hat{v}_{2}\in\mathsf{Val}),\{\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}\})}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{{Biab-Lookup}}

Moreover, to simplify the presentation, we consider an engine without support for predicates; fixing missing-resource errors arising from missing predicates are analogous to fixing missing-resource errors arising from missing heap resources.

All in all, in this setting, the bi-abduction semantics uses a new judgement of the following form:

\hat{\sigma},\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\text{bi}}o:(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},\hat{h})

The judgement is similar to the judgements presented in App.
B
, but additionally returns an anti-heap.

The rules for
\mathsf{consume_{MP}}
and
consCell
are also extended to work appropriately in
bi
mode, with the following judgements:

\begin{array}[]{c}\hat{\sigma}.\textsf{consCell}(\text{bi},\hat{v})=((\hat{v}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}),\hat{h})\\
\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(\text{bi},(P_{i},outs_{i})|_{i=1}^{n},\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow((\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}),\hat{h})\end{array}

Below, we provide the list of rules that significantly change compared to the semantics presented in App.
B
. Rules we do not provide are generally the same as previously, but now return an additional empty anti-heap
\emptyset
.

\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{1},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{v}_{2}\text{ fresh}\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\triangleq\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land\hat{v}_{1}\in\mathsf{Nat}\land\hat{v}_{1}\not\in{\sf dom}(\hat{h})\hskip 8.50012pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})}{(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{E}]\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\text{bi}}\mathit{ok}:((\hat{s}[\mathsf{x}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\land\hat{v}_{2}\in\mathsf{Val}),\{\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}\})}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{Biab-Lookup}}\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{1}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{1},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 8.50012pt\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{2}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}^{\prime}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{2},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{v}_{3}\text{ fresh}\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}\triangleq\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\land\hat{v}_{1}\in\mathsf{Nat}\land\hat{v}_{1}\not\in{\sf dom}(\hat{h})\wedge\hat{v}_{3}\in\mathsf{Val}\hskip 8.50012pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})}{(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),[\mathsf{E}_{1}]\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{E}_{2}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\text{bi}}\mathit{ok}:((\hat{s},\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}),\{\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{3}\})}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{Biab-Mutate}}\frac{\hat{\sigma},\mathsf{C}_{1}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\text{bi}}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},\hat{h}_{1})\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},\mathsf{C}_{2}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\text{bi}}o:(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime},\hat{h}_{2})\hskip 8.50012pt\mathtt{sv}({\sf dom}(\hat{h}_{2}))\cap(\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\setminus\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\sigma}))=\emptyset}{\hat{\sigma},\mathsf{C}_{1};\mathsf{C}_{2}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\text{bi}}o:(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime},\hat{h}_{1}\uplus\hat{h}_{2})}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{Biab-Seq}}

\frac{\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\triangleq\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{v}\in\mathsf{Nat}\land\hat{v}\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\hat{h})\hskip 8.50012pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{v}^{\prime}\text{ fresh}\hskip 8.50012pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land\hat{v}^{\prime}\in\mathsf{Val}}{(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}).\textsf{consCell}(\text{bi},\hat{v})=((\hat{v}^{\prime},(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})),\{\hat{v}\mapsto\hat{v}^{\prime}\})}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{Biab-ConsCell}}\frac{\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(\text{bi},(P,outs),\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow((\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}),\hat{h}_{1})\hskip 8.50012pt\hskip 8.50012pt\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(\text{bi},ps,\hat{\theta}^{\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\rightsquigarrow((\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}),\hat{h}_{2})}{\mathsf{consume_{MP}}(\text{bi},(P,outs)::ps,\hat{\theta},\hat{\sigma})\rightsquigarrow((\hat{\theta}^{\prime\prime},\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}),\hat{h}_{1}\uplus\hat{h}_{2})}\hskip 8.50012pt\textsc{{Biab-MacMP-Seq}}

F.2
Soundness Proofs

To prove Thm.
8
, we need the following lemma:

Lemma F.1

(Symbolic variables of codomains of anti-heaps)
.

If
\hat{\sigma},\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\text{bi}}o:(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},\hat{h})
, then
\mathtt{sv}({\sf codom}(\hat{h}))\subseteq(\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\setminus\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\sigma}))
; which moreover implies
\mathtt{sv}({\sf codom}(\hat{h}))\cap\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\sigma})=\emptyset
.

We also need the following frame result:

Theorem F.2
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\hat{\sigma},\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{m}o:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\land(\mathtt{sv}(\hat{h}_{f})\cap(\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\setminus\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\sigma}))=\emptyset)\implies\hat{\sigma}\cdot\hat{h}_{f},\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{m}o:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{h}_{f}\end{array}

We now present the main proof:

Proof of Thm.
8
.

The proof is by induction. We show some illustrative case for fixes.

(rule
Biab-Lookup
)

From the application of the rule, we have:

\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{1},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime}\land\hat{v}_{1}\in\mathsf{Nat}\land\hat{v}_{1}\not\in{\sf dom}(\hat{h})\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{v}_{2}\text{ fresh}}{(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{E}]\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\text{bi}}\mathit{ok}:((\hat{s}[\mathsf{x}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\land\hat{v}_{2}\in\mathsf{Val}),\{\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}\})}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{{Biab-Lookup}}

Define
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}=\hat{v}_{1}\in\mathsf{Nat}\land\hat{v}_{1}\not\in{\sf dom}(\hat{h})\land\hat{\pi}^{\prime}
,
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}=(\hat{s}[\mathsf{x}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
and
\hat{h}=\{\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}\}
.

Notice that now
Lookup
with
\hat{\sigma}\cdot\hat{h}=(\hat{s},\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})
:

\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{1},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{h}(\hat{v}_{1})=\hat{v}_{2}\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})}{(\hat{s},\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}),\mathsf{x}\mathtt{\ :=\ }[\mathsf{E}]\Downarrow_{\Gamma}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{s}[\mathsf{x}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{{Lookup}}

and the result follows.

(rule
Biab-Mutate
)

From the application of the rule we have:

\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{1}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{1},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{2}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}^{\prime}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{2},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{v}_{3}\text{ fresh}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\land\hat{v}_{1}\in\mathsf{Nat}\land\hat{v}_{1}\not\in{\sf dom}(\hat{h})\wedge\hat{v}_{3}\in\mathsf{Val}\hskip 18.49988pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime})}{(\hat{s},\hat{h},\hat{\pi}),[\mathsf{E}_{1}]\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{E}_{2}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\text{bi}}\mathit{ok}:((\hat{s},\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}],\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}),\{\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{3}\})}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{{Biab-Mutate}}

Now we can apply the rule
Mutate
with
\hat{\sigma}\cdot[\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{3}]=(\hat{s},\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{3}],\hat{\pi}^{*})
where
\hat{\pi}^{*}=\hat{\pi}\wedge\hat{v}_{1}\in\mathsf{Nat}\land\hat{v}_{1}\not\in{\sf dom}(\hat{h})\wedge\hat{v}_{3}\in\mathsf{Val}

\frac{\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{1}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}^{*}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{1},\hat{\pi}^{\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\llbracket\mathsf{E}_{2}\rrbracket_{\hat{s}}^{\hat{\pi}^{\prime}}\Downarrow(\hat{v}_{2},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{h}(\hat{v}_{1})=\hat{v}_{3}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\texttt{SAT}(\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})\hskip 18.49988pt\hat{h}^{\prime}=\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{2}]}{(\hat{s},\hat{h}[\hat{v}_{1}\mapsto\hat{v}_{3}],\hat{\pi}^{*}),[\mathsf{E}_{1}]\mathtt{\ :=\ }\mathsf{E}_{2}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{s},\hat{h}^{\prime},\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime})}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{{Mutate}}

and
\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime\prime}=\hat{\pi}^{\prime\prime}\wedge\hat{v}_{1}\in\mathsf{Nat}\land\hat{v}_{1}\not\in{\sf dom}(\hat{h})\wedge\hat{v}_{3}\in\mathsf{Val}
, which gives the result.

(rule
Biab-Seq
)

From the application of the rule, we have:

\frac{\hat{\sigma},\mathsf{C}_{1}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\text{bi}}\mathit{ok}:(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},\hat{h}_{1})\hskip 18.49988pt\hat{\sigma}^{\prime},\mathsf{C}_{2}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\text{bi}}o:(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime},\hat{h}_{2})\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\mathtt{sv}({\sf dom}(\hat{h}_{2}))\cap(\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime})\setminus\mathtt{sv}(\hat{\sigma}))=\emptyset}{\hat{\sigma},\mathsf{C}_{1};\mathsf{C}_{2}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\text{bi}}o:(\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime},\hat{h}_{1}\uplus\hat{h}_{2})}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{{Biab-Seq}}

By the induction hypothesis,
\hat{\sigma}\cdot\hat{h}_{1},\mathsf{C}_{1}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{UX}}\mathit{ok}:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}
and
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{h}_{2},\mathsf{C}_{2}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{UX}}o:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}
where
\hat{h}_{1}
and
\hat{h}_{2}
are the antiframes for
\mathsf{C}_{1}
and
\mathsf{C}_{2}
, respectively.

From
\hat{\sigma}\cdot\hat{h}_{1},\mathsf{C}_{1}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{UX}}\mathit{ok}:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}
, Lem.
F.1
, and Thm.
F.2
, we get
\hat{\sigma}\cdot\hat{h}_{1}\cdot\hat{h}_{2},\mathsf{C}_{1}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{UX}}\mathit{ok}:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{h}_{2}
.

Now we can apply rule
Seq
:

\frac{\hat{\sigma}\cdot\hat{h}_{1}\cdot\hat{h}_{2},\mathsf{C}_{1}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{UX}}\mathit{ok}:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{h}_{2}\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}\cdot\hat{h}_{2},\mathsf{C}_{2}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{UX}}o:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}{\hat{\sigma}\cdot\hat{h}_{1}\cdot\hat{h}_{2},\mathsf{C}_{1}\Downarrow_{\Gamma}^{\textsf{UX}}o:\hat{\sigma}^{\prime\prime}}\hskip 9.24994pt\textsc{{Seq}}

and the result follows.

∎

Appendix G
Applications: Correctness

The following lemma is convenient for our proofs, it, like Lem.
D.2
, follows easily from the fact that the concrete language is compositional, this time with respect to stores:

Lemma G.1

(Specification Validity with Restrictions)
.

\begin{array}[]{l}\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {\mathsf{C}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\}}\iff\\
\hskip 9.24994pt(\forall\theta,s,h,o,s^{\prime},h^{\prime}.~\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\theta)\subseteq\mathtt{lv}(P)\land\mathtt{pv}(s)\subseteq\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{C})\land\\
\hskip 18.49988pt\theta,(s,h)\models P\land(s,h),\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\implies\\
\hskip 18.49988pt(o\neq\mathit{miss}\land\theta,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\models Q_{o}))\\
\gamma\models{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}P\end{array}\right]}\ {\mathsf{C}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{ok}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right]}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left[\mathit{err}:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right]}\iff\\
\hskip 9.24994pt(\forall\theta,s^{\prime},h^{\prime},o.~\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\theta)\subseteq\mathtt{lv}(Q_{o})\land\mathtt{pv}(s^{\prime})\subseteq\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{C})\land\theta,(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})\models Q_{o}\implies\\
\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 9.24994pt(\exists s,h\ldotp~\theta,(s,h)\models P~\land~(s,h),\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})))\end{array}

G.1
Correctness of
verifyOX

Theorem G.2

(Correctness of
verifyOX
)
.

Given a function
f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\,\{\,\mathsf{C};\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{E}\,\}
, a function specification
s={\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\end{array}\right\}}\ {f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}\end{array}\right\}}
, and an OX valid environment
\models(\gamma,\Gamma)
, where
f\not\in{\sf dom}(\gamma)
: let
\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma[f\mapsto s]
, if
\texttt{verifyOX}(\Gamma,f,s)
, then
\models(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime})
, where
\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma[f\mapsto(\vec{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{C},\mathsf{E})]
.

Proof.

We assume some
\gamma
,
\Gamma
,
\gamma^{\prime}
,
\Gamma^{\prime}
,

,
\vec{\mathsf{x}}
,

,

,

(A1)

is an external function specification

(A2)

f\notin\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\gamma)

(A3)

\models(\gamma,\Gamma)

(A4)

\gamma^{\prime}=\gamma[f\mapsto(\vec{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{C}_{f},\mathsf{E})]

(A5)

\Gamma^{\prime}=\Gamma[f\mapsto t]

and we aim to show that
\models(\gamma^{\prime},\Gamma^{\prime})
, i.e.

\begin{array}[]{l}\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\Gamma^{\prime})\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\gamma^{\prime})~\land\\
(\forall f,\vec{\mathsf{x}},\mathsf{C},\mathsf{E},S.~f(\vec{\mathsf{x}})\{\mathsf{C};\mathtt{return}\ \mathsf{E}\}\in\gamma^{\prime}\land S\in\Gamma^{\prime}(f)\implies\exists S^{\prime}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f}(S).~\gamma^{\prime}\models\mathsf{C}:S^{\prime})\end{array}

(A3-5) imply
\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\Gamma^{\prime})\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\gamma^{\prime})
and for all functions that are already in
\gamma
, the statement follows straightforwardly from (A3). The only non-trivial case is the newly added function
f
and its specification

. (A1) and the definition of Internalisation imply that any internal specification of

has the precondition

, where
\mathsf{x}
are the arguments of

as defined in (A4) and

are the remaining program variables of

.

We therefore assume some
\theta,s,h,s^{\prime}
and

such that

, where

(H1b)

(H1c)

(H1d)

\mathtt{pv}(s)\subseteq\mathtt{pv}(\mathsf{C})

(H1e)

\mathop{\mathrm{dom}}(\theta)\subseteq\mathtt{pv}(\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P)
.

(H2)

(s,h),\mathsf{C}\Downarrow_{\gamma^{\prime}}o:(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})

To avoid some clutter later, we also define
(H3a)

\sigma=(s,h)
and
(H3b)

\sigma^{\prime}=(s^{\prime},h^{\prime})
.

The execution of
\texttt{verifyOX}(\Gamma,f,s)
implies the following definitions and properties:

.

.

of

\forall\varepsilon,\hat{\theta},\hat{s},h,P\ldotp\varepsilon(\hat{\theta}),(\emptyset,h)\models P\implies\\

.

Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\triangleq Q_{\mathit{ok}}[\mathsf{E}/\mathsf{ret}]
and show that it is an OX-internalisation of

:

\vec{\mathsf{p}}=\mathtt{pv}(Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime})

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow~

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow~

\displaystyle\exists\vec{v}\subset\mathsf{Val}\ldotp~\bar{\theta}[\vec{p}\mapsto\vec{v}],(\bar{s},\bar{h})\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}\mathrel{\star}\mathsf{ret}=\mathsf{E}[\vec{p}/\vec{\mathsf{p}}]

\displaystyle\Rightarrow~

\displaystyle\Rightarrow~

\displaystyle\bar{\theta},(\bar{s},\bar{h})\models Q_{\mathit{ok}}

where the last implications holds because
\vec{p}
are fresh w.r.t

(since they are fresh w.r.t.

).

{\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}\vec{\mathsf{x}}=\vec{x}\mathrel{\star}P\mathrel{\star}\vec{\mathsf{z}}=\mathtt{null}\end{array}\right\}}\ {}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{ok:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{ok}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}}\ {\color[rgb]{0,0,1}\left\{err:\begin{array}[]{@{}c@{}}Q_{\mathit{err}}^{\prime}\end{array}\right\}}\in\mathit{Int}_{\gamma^{\prime},f}^{OX}(t).
